Soft and virtual corrections to semi-inclusive DIS up to four loops in QCD
Saurav Goyal, Sven-Olaf Moch, Vaibhav Pathak, Narayan Rana, V. Ravindran

Abstract
We apply the threshold resummation formalism for semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS) to derive the soft and virtual corrections for the SIDIS cross section up to four loops in QCD. Using the recently computed next-to-next-to-leading order QCD corrections for the SIDIS cross section together with known results for the form factor and splitting functions in QCD up to four loops, we derive the complete soft and collinear contributions to the SIDIS coefficient functions at four-loop order. We also include systematically the next-to-leading power corrections, which are suppressed near threshold. The numerical analysis of the new four-loop corrections shows a small effect on the cross section underpinning the very good perturbative stability of the SIDIS process at that order in perturbation theory, including the reduced dependence on the renormalization and factorization scales μ R \mu_{R} and μ F \mu_{F} .



DESY 25-079

I
Introduction

The semi-inclusive deep-inelastic scattering (SIDIS) process
Aschenauer et al. 2019
, which involves the scattering of a lepton off an initial hadron P and the production of an identified hadron H
′

in the final state, is a vital experimental tool in probing the internal structure of hadrons. This process is particularly important for studying parton distribution functions (PDFs) and fragmentation functions (FFs) through Quantum Chromodynamics (QCD).

On the theoretical side,
the earliest next-to-leading order (NLO) QCD results for SIDIS are from a seminal paper
Altarelli et al. 1979
, see also

Nason and Webber 1994
;
Furmanski and Petronzio 1982
;
Graudenz 1994
;
de Florian and Rotstein Habarnau 2013
;
de Florian and Rotstein Habarnau 2013
.
Recently, the QCD results at next-to-next-to-leading order (NNLO) accuracy for both spin independent and spin dependent SIDIS cross sections were obtained independently by two groups
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
.
In QCD factorization, which provides the backbone of the perturbative framework, the fixed order results for the coefficient functions (CFs), parameterizing the hard scattering, often provide reliable predictions for wide range of kinematic variables.
However, they fail in the threshold regions, because of
the presence of large logarithms. In the case of SIDIS, these logarithms originate from the partonic configurations where partons are soft and/or collinear to each other.
Although the CFs in SIDIS are infrared-safe, meaning that soft and collinear divergences cancel out, these cancellations lead to certain distributions in CFs represented by
distributions such as
\delta(1-\xi)

and

\displaystyle{\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{\xi}}=\Bigg[\frac{\ln^{j}(1-\xi)}{1-\xi}\Bigg]_{+}\,,\quad j=0,1,\cdots

(1)

where
\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
are appropriate scaling variables.
We call them soft plus virtual (SV) contributions, and their importance was recognized early on, leading to several improvements in QCD perturbation theory, see
Daleo and Sassot 2003
;
Daleo et al. 2003
;
Anderle et al. 2013a
;
Anderle et al. 2017
.
Currently, approximations at (next-to-)
3
-leading order (N
3
LO) accuracy taking into account only threshold contributions, also called SV and threshold resummation to (next-to-)
3
-leading logarithmic (N
3
LL) accuracy are available
Abele et al. 2021
;
Abele et al. 2022
;
Anderle et al. 2013a
,
improving upon earlier results
Cacciari and Catani 2001
;
Anderle et al. 2013b
;
Sterman and Vogelsang 2006
at next-to-leading logarithmic (NLL) accuracy.
These achievements were possible thanks to an intriguing connection between SIDIS and the rapidity distributions of pair of leptons in the Drell-Yan (DY) process,
Anderle et al. 2013a
;
Westmark and Owens 2017
;
Banerjee et al. 2018a
.
Beyond the SV contributions, one encounters terms with logarithmic enhancement of the type
\ln^{j}(1-\xi)
, for
j=0,1,\cdots
, which are classified as next-to-leading power (NLP) corrections or next-to-SV (NSV) terms.
Threshold resummation provides a framework to resum these large logarithms, i.e. the SV terms in eq. (
1
) and NSV terms, to all order orders in perturbation theory to obtain improved phenomenological predictions.
In this paper, we push threshold resummation for the SIDIS CFs to N
4
LL accuracy and provide their approximations at N
4
LO in QCD, accounting for both SV as well as NSV terms in a systematic procedure.

The outline of the article is as follows:
In Sec.
II
, we present the theory framework for the SIDIS process, recall the basics of mass factorization in QCD and set-up the formalism, that allows to resum the threshold enhanced SV and NSV contributions.
The new fixed-order approximate SV and NSV results for the coefficient functions at N
3
LO and N
4
LO and
are presented in Sec.
III
.
The explicit form of the exponential for the resummation of the threshold logarithms is presented in
Sec.
IV
in Mellin space as a function of complex valued
N_{i},i=1,2
, conjugate to
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
respectively.
An alternative approach to the threshold expansion through the physical evolution kernel is presented in
Sec.
V
, while phenomenological studies are performed with Sec.
VI
.
We summarize and present our conclusions in Sec.
VII
.
Relevant formulae for the threshold resummation are collected in the Appendices.

II
Theory

The SIDIS process is given by

\displaystyle l(k_{l})+\text{P}(P)\rightarrow l({k}^{\prime}_{l})+\text{H}(P_{H})+\text{X}\,,

(2)

where the incoming (outgoing) lepton
l
has momentum
k_{l}
(
{k}^{\prime}_{l}
),
and
P
(
H
) is the incoming (outgoing) hadron with momentum
P
(
P_{H}
).
The remaining set of final state particles is denoted by
X
.

We consider two types of differential observables namely, the spin independent cross section
where
we average over the spins of incoming particles and sum the spins of final state particles:

\displaystyle d\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\sum_{s_{l},S,s^{\prime}_{l},S_{H}}d\sigma_{s_{l},S}^{s^{\prime}_{l},S_{H}}\,,

(3)

and the corresponding spin-dependent cross section,

\displaystyle d\Delta\sigma={1\over 2}\sum_{s^{\prime}_{l},S_{H}}\left(d\sigma_{s_{l}=\frac{1}{2},S=\frac{1}{2}}^{s^{\prime}_{l},S_{H}}-d\sigma_{s_{l}=\frac{1}{2},S=-\frac{1}{2}}^{s^{\prime}_{l},S_{H}}\right)\,,

(4)

where
s_{l}
,
S
are the spins of incoming lepton and hadron,
and
s^{\prime}_{l}
,
S_{H}
the spins of the outgoing ones, respectively.
The cross section depends on
the space-like momentum transfer
Q^{2}=-q^{2}
where
q={k}_{l}-{k}^{\prime}_{l}
,
the Bjorken variable
x=Q^{2}/2P\cdot q
,
the inelasticity
y={P\cdot q}/{P\cdot k_{l}}
,
and the scaling variable
z={P\cdot P_{H}}/{P\cdot q}
, the fraction of the initial energy transferred to the final-state hadron.

We restrict ourselves to scattering events where there is only photon exchange between the incoming lepton and the hadron. We find that
the SIDIS cross section factorizes into a leptonic part and a hadronic part.
The leptonic part is simple to calculate, whereas the hadronic part encapsulates the physics of the strong interactions and is challenging due to the compositeness of both the incoming and outgoing hadrons.
However, using parity and time reversal invariance and electromagnetic current conservation, we can parametrize the hadronic
part of the SIDIS cross section in terms of structure functions.
For example, for spin averaged case, we have two structure functions,
F_{J},J=1,2
. The structure functions are Lorentz invariant, depend on the hadronic scaling variables
x,z
and, when higher order quantum corrections are included, also on the scale
Q^{2}
of the virtual momemtum transferred.
We find

\frac{d^{3}\sigma}{dxdydz}=\frac{4\pi\alpha_{e}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\left[yF_{1}(x,z,Q^{2})+\frac{(1-y)}{y}F_{2}(x,z,Q^{2})\right]\,\!,

(5)

where
\alpha_{e}=e^{2}/(4\pi)
is the fine structure constant and
e
is the electric charge. In the literature, one often parametrises the hadronic tensor in terms of structure functions denoted by
W_{J}

(J=1,2)
and
the
F_{J}
are related to
W_{J}
through

W_{1}=F_{1}/M
and
W_{2}=F_{2}/(yE)
with
the energy
E
of the incoming lepton and
M
being mass of incoming hadron.
The spin-dependent cross section for longitudinally polarized hadrons in the initial state depends only on one structure function,
g_{1}
,

\frac{d^{3}\Delta\sigma}{dxdydz}=\frac{4\pi\alpha_{e}^{2}}{Q^{2}}\big(2-y\big)g_{1}(x,z,Q^{2})\,.

(6)

In perturbative QCD, the structure functions factorise as a convolution of (spin-dependent) PDFs,
(\Delta)f_{a/\text{P}}(x_{1},\mu_{F}^{2})
and FFs,
D_{\text{H}^{\prime}/b}(z_{1},\mu_{F}^{2})

with (spin-dependent) CFs,
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}(x/x_{1},z/z_{1},Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2})
, the latter being calculable in perturbation theory.

\displaystyle\big(g_{1}\big)F_{J}(x,z,Q^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{a,b=q,\overline{q},g}\int_{x}^{1}\frac{dx_{1}}{x_{1}}(\Delta)f_{a/\text{P}}\left(x_{1},\mu_{F}^{2}\right)\int_{z}^{1}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}D_{\text{H}^{\prime}/b}\left(z_{1},\mu_{F}^{2}\right)(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}\left(\frac{x}{x_{1}},\frac{z}{z_{1}},Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2}\right)\,.

(7)

Here,
\mu_{F}
is the factorisation scale,
x_{1}
is the incoming parton’s momentum fraction with respect to the hadron P, i.e.
x_{1}=p_{a}/P

and
z_{1}
is the momentum fraction of the final state parton
b
carried away by the outgoing hadron H, i.e.
z_{1}=P_{H}/p_{b}
.
Note also, that the spin-dependent PDFs are given by

\Delta f_{a/\text{P}}=f_{a(\uparrow)/\text{P}(\uparrow)}-f_{a(\downarrow)/\text{P}(\uparrow)}
, where arrows denote the polarisations of respective particles with respect to beam director.

SIDIS coefficient functions (CFs) are derived from parton subprocesses via mass factorization, neglecting quark mass effects. Collinear divergences arise when massless partons become collinear, such as an incoming or outgoing parton with others produced in the hard scattering. Thanks to QCD factorization, the bare (spin-dependent) CFs
(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

in dimensional regularization
(d=4+\varepsilon)
factorize through Altarelli-Parisi kernels at the scale
\mu_{F}
.

\displaystyle(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Delta)\Gamma_{c\leftarrow a}(x^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\varepsilon)\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,cd}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\varepsilon)\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{b\leftarrow d}(z^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\varepsilon)

(8)

The kernels
\Gamma_{c\leftarrow a}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{b\leftarrow d}
are given in terms of space- and time-like splitting functions and summarize the collinear divergences through poles in
\varepsilon
, see, e.g.
Goyal et al. 2025
.
The partonic scaling variables are
x^{\prime}=x/x_{1}
and
z^{\prime}=z/z_{1}
and the convolutions
\otimes
and
\tilde{\otimes}
between the functions
A(x^{\prime})
,
C(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
and
B(z^{\prime})
are defined as follows:

\displaystyle A(x^{\prime})\otimes C(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\tilde{\otimes}B(z^{\prime})\,=\,

(9)

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}}^{1}\frac{dx_{1}}{x_{1}}\int_{z^{\prime}}^{1}\frac{dz_{1}}{z_{1}}A(x_{1})C\left(\frac{x^{\prime}}{x_{1}},\frac{z^{\prime}}{z_{1}}\right)B(z_{1})\,.

The collinear finite CFs
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}
in eq. (
8
) are expanded in powers of strong coupling constant
a_{s}=\alpha_{s}/(4\pi)
at the renormalisation scale
\mu_{R}
,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2})\,=\,\delta_{ab}\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})

(10)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{R}^{2})(\Delta){\cal C}^{(i)}_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\mu_{R}^{2})\,.

The CFs
(\Delta){\cal C}^{(i)}_{J,ab}
in general contain distributions
\delta(1-\xi)
and
{\cal D}^{i}_{\overline{\xi}}
, cf. eq. (
1
), where
\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
and regular functions of
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
.
We can write
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}
as,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}=\sum_{r}(\Delta)C_{J,ab}^{r}h_{r}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})+\sum_{\beta}\left((\Delta)C_{J,ab,x^{\prime}}^{\beta}(z^{\prime})X_{\beta}(x^{\prime})+(\Delta)C_{J,ab,z^{\prime}}^{\beta}(x^{\prime})Z_{\beta}(z^{\prime})\right)+(\Delta)R_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\,,

(11)

where
r=\{\delta_{x}\delta_{z},\delta_{x}j,j\delta_{z},jk\}
with
j,k=0,1,2
etc and

\displaystyle h_{\delta_{x}\delta_{z}}

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle h_{\delta_{x}j}

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x^{\prime}){\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},

\displaystyle h_{j\delta_{z}}

\displaystyle={\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\delta(1-z^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle h_{jk}

\displaystyle={\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}{\cal D}^{k}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}

(12)

and
\beta=\{\delta_{x},\delta_{z},j\}
with
j=0,1,2
etc

\displaystyle X_{\delta_{x}}

\displaystyle=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle Z_{\delta_{x}}

\displaystyle=\delta(1-z^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle X_{j}

\displaystyle={\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\,,

\displaystyle Z_{j}

\displaystyle={\cal D}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\,.

(13)

The functions
(\Delta)R_{J,ab}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
contain all remaining terms that are regular (no distributions).
Furthermore, if we expand the CFs
(\Delta)C^{\beta}_{J,ab,x^{\prime}}
and
(\Delta)C^{\beta}_{J,ab,z^{\prime}}
in eq. (
11
) around
x^{\prime}=1
and
z^{\prime}=1
,
we obtain a series of the form
(1-\xi)^{i}\ln^{j}(1-\xi),\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
for
i,j=0,1
etc.
The leading terms in this series (
i=0
) are the so-called NSV terms, while the rest with powers
i>0
are denoted as beyond-NSV (BNSV) terms.
In this notation, the CFs in eq. (
11
) read

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}=\delta_{ab}\Big((\Delta){\cal C}_{J}^{\text{SV}}\Big)+(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}^{\text{NSV}}+(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}^{\text{BNSV}}\,,

(14)

where the SV term does not receive any contributions if
a=b=g
.
Each term in the above equation can be expanded in powers series of
a_{s}
as,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J}^{\text{SV}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Delta)C_{J,qq}^{(0),\delta_{x}\delta_{z}}h_{\delta_{x}\delta_{z}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{n}\Bigg[(\Delta)C_{J,qq}^{(n),\delta_{x}\delta_{z}}h_{\delta_{x}\delta_{z}}+\sum_{j=0}^{2n-1}(\Delta)C_{J,qq}^{(n),\delta_{x}j}h_{\delta_{x}j}+\sum_{j=0}^{2n-1}(\Delta)C_{J,qq}^{(n),j\delta_{z}}h_{j\delta_{z}}+\sum_{j=0}^{2n-2}\sum_{k=0}^{2n-2-j}(\Delta)C_{J,qq}^{(n),jk}h_{jk}\Bigg]\,,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J}^{\text{NSV}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{n}\Bigg[\sum_{j=0}^{2n-1}\Big((\Delta)C_{J}^{(n),\delta_{x}j}X_{\delta_{x}}L_{j}(z^{\prime})+(\Delta)C_{J}^{(n),j\delta_{z}}L_{j}(x^{\prime})Z_{\delta_{z}}\Big)

(15)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=0}^{2n-2}\sum_{k=0}^{2n-2-j}\Big((\Delta)C_{J}^{(n),jk}X_{j}L_{k}(z^{\prime})+(\Delta)C_{J}^{(n),jk}L_{j}(x^{\prime})Z_{k}\Big)\Bigg]\,,

where
L_{j}(\xi)=\ln^{j}(1-\xi),\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
.
Schematically,
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J}^{\text{SV}}
contains double distributions (
h_{r}
) given in eq. (
II
),
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}^{\text{NSV}}
contains single distribution in
x^{\prime}
or
z^{\prime}
(
X_{\beta},Z_{\beta}
) each multiplied by powers of
\ln^{j}(1-z^{\prime})
or
\ln^{j}(1-x^{\prime})

respectively, where
j=0,1,2
etc.
Any regular terms in
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
that do not contain any distributions fall within the BNSV categrory.
The NNLO results in
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
for the CFs

(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}^{\text{SV}}
are in complete agreement with SV predictions for unpolarised structure functions
F_{1,2}

Abele et al. 2021
, see also
Ravindran et al. 2007
;
Ahmed et al. 2014a
.
The leading terms of the NSV contributions agree as well with ref.
Abele et al. 2021
, where the dominant NLP terms have been presented.

The first principle’s derivation of the threshold enhanced logarithms in eq. (
II
) exploits the renormalisation group equations and the factorization of soft and collinear singularities to all orders in QCD, building on well-known developments for inclusive cross sections such as DIS, DY and Higgs boson production (or, more generally, colorless
n
-particle production), see e.g.
Sterman 1987
;
Catani and Trentadue 1989
;
Moch and Vogt 2005
;
Moch et al. 2005a
;
Ravindran 2006a
;
Ravindran 2006b
;
de Florian and Mazzitelli 2012
;
Bonvini 2012
;
Ahmed et al. 2014b
;
Kumar et al. 2015
;
Ahmed et al. 2014c
;
Catani et al. 2014
.
For observables in differential kinematics, where the threshold limit is approached in terms of two different scaling variables,
such as the rapidity distributions of lepton pairs in the DY process or Higgs boson production, threshold logarithms have also been systematically resumed to all orders in perturbation theory, see
Laenen et al. 2008
;
Laenen et al. 2011
;
Bonocore et al. 2015a
;
Bonocore et al. 2015b
;
Bonocore et al. 2016
;
Del Duca et al. 2017
;
Bahjat-Abbas et al. 2019
;
Soar et al. 2010
;
Moch and Vogt 2009
;
de Florian et al. 2014
;
Beneke et al. 2019
;
Beneke et al. 2020a
;
Beneke et al. 2020b
.
In addition, both single as well as double variable resummations were extended for flavour diagonal channels to include NSV contributions
Catani and Trentadue 1989
;
Ravindran et al. 2007
;
Ravindran and Smith 2007
;
Banerjee et al. 2018b
;
Banerjee et al. 2018a
;
Ahmed et al. 2014a
.

The kinematics in these cases, is similar to the SIDIS process, where the limits
x^{\prime}\rightarrow 1
and
z^{\prime}\rightarrow 1
are considered.
In the following we develop the formalism to resum both SV as well as NSV logarithms of flavour diagonal contributions, namely
(\Delta)\mathcal{C}_{J,qq}
to all orders both in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space as well in their corresponding Mellin space
(N_{1},N_{2})
.
The reason to consider only flavour diagonal channels is because of their simple collinear factorisation structure.
The starting point is the mass factorised form of the bare CFs in eq. (
8
) in
d=4+\varepsilon
dimensions,
expanding the sum over the parton channels,

\displaystyle(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qq}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)=

\displaystyle~(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,q\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qg}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow g}

(16)

\displaystyle+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}q}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}g}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow g}

\displaystyle+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,gq}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,g\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,gg}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow g}\,.

The convolutions with the space- and time-like AP kernels
\Gamma_{a\leftarrow b}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{a\leftarrow b}
can be distinguished as follows: i) terms involving only diagonal combinations, such as
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}
;
ii) contributions involving a product of one diagonal with a pair of non-diagonal terms, for example
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qg}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow g}
.
The terms in i) contain SV plus NSV terms when the convolutions have been performed
while the latter ones in case ii) will be composed beyond NSV terms only.
Note that the diagonal AP kernels
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}
contain convolutions among diagonal splitting functions,
(\Delta)P_{qq}
and
\tilde{P}_{qq}
respectively,
or one diagonal and a pair of off-diagonal ones,
(\Delta)P_{ab},\tilde{P}_{ab},a\not=b
.
We drop those terms in
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}
(
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}
), that contain a pair of off-diagonal splitting functions
(\Delta)P_{ab}
(
\tilde{P}_{ab}
), as they contribute only beyond NSV accuracy.
In addition, we drop all the BNSV terms in the diagonal splitting functions as well.
This leads to

\displaystyle\!(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qq}^{\text{SV+NSV}}\!\!=\!(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\!\!\otimes\!(\Delta)\mathcal{C}_{J,qq}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\,.

Here, the superscript on the AP kernels indicate their truncation to SV and NSV terms only.
In this way we can write the bare CFs in eq. (
8
) are given in terms of only diagonal quantities, i.e.,
(\Delta)\hat{\mathcal{C}}_{J,qq}
,
(\Delta){\mathcal{C}}_{J,qq}
and AP kernels
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}
,
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}
, where BNSV terms and the sum
ab
over the parton channels are dropped.

The bare flavor off-diagonal CFs instead contain both diagonal and off-diagonal terms from
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}
and AP kernels.
For instance
(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qg}
reads

\displaystyle(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qg}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)=

\displaystyle~(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,q\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qg}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow g}

(18)

\displaystyle+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}q}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{\overline{q}\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,\overline{q}g}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow g}

\displaystyle+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,gq}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow q}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,g\overline{q}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow\overline{q}}+(\Delta)\Gamma_{g\leftarrow q}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,gg}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{g\leftarrow g}\,.

Unlike the diagonal case in eq. (
16
), no single term provides pure SV contributions in the threshold limit.
Hence, the mass factorised result for the off-diagonal channel contains NSV terms only and those beyond, where the former ones come from contributions that contain at least two diagonal quantities, either from
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,ab}
or
(\Delta)\Gamma_{a\leftarrow b},\tilde{\Gamma}_{a\leftarrow b}
.
Dropping all terms that contain more than two off-diagonal quantities in the mass factorisation formula, we obtain

\displaystyle(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}^{\text{NSV}}_{J,qg}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\Delta)\Gamma_{qq}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\otimes(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{\overline{q}g}^{\mathrm{NSV}}

(19)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle(\Delta)\Gamma_{qq}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\!\otimes\!(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qg}^{\mathrm{NSV}}\!\tilde{\otimes}\tilde{\Gamma}_{gg}^{\mathrm{SV+NSV}}\,,\qquad

which receives contribution from both CFs,
(\Delta){\cal{C}}_{J,qg}
and
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}
,
unlike the diagonal channel in eq. (
II
) which is directly proportional to
(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qq}
.

The simple structure in the diagonal channel makes it easy for us to study its all order structure of SV and NSV logarithms.
We achieve this by setting up a homogeneous differential equations for CFs in terms of the soft and collinear anomalous dimensions.
The presence of inhomogeneous terms in corresponding differential equations for the off-diagonal channel poses
technical challenges when solving them. Hence, in the rest of the paper, we will focuss only to diagonal partonic channels.

We begin with the eq. (
II
) for the diagonal CFs and rewrite as

\displaystyle(\Delta)\mathcal{C}_{J,qq}^{\rm{SV+NSV}}=\left|\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\right|^{2}\Big((\Delta)\Gamma\Big)^{-1}_{q\leftarrow q}(x^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\otimes(\Delta)\hat{\cal S}_{J,q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)\tilde{\otimes}\Big(\tilde{\Gamma}\Big)^{-1}_{q\leftarrow q}(z^{\prime},\mu_{F}^{2},\varepsilon)

(20)

where
\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)
is the massles quark form factor (FF) in dimensional regularization
and
(\Delta)\hat{\cal S}_{J,q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)
, called soft function, is defined by

\displaystyle(\Delta)\hat{\cal S}_{J,q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\frac{(\Delta)\hat{\cal C}_{J,qq}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)}{\left|\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\right|^{2}}

(21)

Note that in the absence of any real radiation, the soft functions is constrained by kinematics and takes the simple form:
(\Delta)\hat{\cal S}_{J,q}|_{\text{no radiation}}=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})
.

The vector form factor
\hat{F}
has been subject to extensive studies, see, e.g.
Sen 1981
;
Collins 1989
;
Magnea and Sterman 1990
;
Magnea 2001
;
Sterman and Tejeda-Yeomans 2003
;
Moch et al. 2005b
;
Ravindran 2006a
.
In massless QCD and within dimensional regularization it exhibits IR and UV singularities as poles in
\varepsilon
.
The UV divergences can be removed by renormalisation of the strong coupling, while the soft and collinear, i.e., IR
sigularities, factorise as

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)=Z_{\hat{F}_{q}}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},\varepsilon)F_{q,fin}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},\varepsilon)\,,

(22)

where
Z_{\hat{F}_{q}}
is IR singular and
F_{q,fin}
is IR finite, the scale
\mu_{s}
is IR factorisation scale.
Renormalisation group invariance implies that

\displaystyle\mu_{s}^{2}\frac{d}{d\mu_{s}^{2}}\ln Z_{\hat{F}_{q}}(\mu_{s}^{2})=\gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}(\mu_{s}^{2})\,,

(23)

where
\gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}
to all order in
a_{s}
takes the form

\displaystyle\gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}={1\over 2}\Bigg(-A^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\ln\Bigg(\frac{Q^{2}}{\mu_{s}^{2}}\Bigg)+2B^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})+f^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\Bigg)\,.

(24)

Here,
A^{q},B^{q},f^{q}
denote the cusp (
A^{q}
), the quark virtual
B^{q}
and the eikomal anomalous dimensions, which all have expansions in powers of the strong coupling as
A^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})=\sum_{i}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{s}^{2})A^{q}_{i}
etc.
They are currently known to four-loop accuracy in QCD
Henn et al. 2020
;
von Manteuffel et al. 2020
;
Das et al. 2020a
;
Das et al. 2020b
;
Agarwal et al. 2021
;
Lee et al. 2021
;
Moch et al. 2024
;
Kniehl et al. 2025
.
Thus, the solution to eq. (
23
) for
Z_{\hat{F}_{q}}

in the
\overline{\text{MS}}
scheme contains only poles in
\varepsilon
whose coefficients are completely controlled by universal anomalous dimensions
A^{q},B^{q}
and
f^{q}
and the coefficients of QCD
\beta
function, the latter being known even to five loops
Baikov et al. 2017
;
Herzog et al. 2017
;
Luthe et al. 2017
;
Chetyrkin et al. 2017
.

The finite part
F_{q,fin}
of the form factor is subject to a differential equation with respect to
Q^{2}
,

\displaystyle Q^{2}\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}\ln\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)=\Gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\,,

(25)

where

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(K_{d}^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2},\varepsilon)+G_{d}^{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},\varepsilon)\bigg)\,.

(26)

The kernel
K_{d}^{q}=2d\ln Z_{\hat{F}_{q}}/d\ln(Q^{2})
contains entire IR singular terms while the function
G_{d}^{q}=2d\ln F_{q,fin}/d\ln(Q^{2})
is IR finite by definition.
Since, we have defined the form factor in dimensional regularisation, the solution to the
K+G
equation
takes the simple form

\displaystyle\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)=\exp\left(\int_{0}^{Q^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}\Gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}(\lambda^{2},\varepsilon)\right)\,,

(27)

with
\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2}=0,\varepsilon)=1
as boundary condition.
Since all IR poles in
\varepsilon
are contained in
K_{d}^{q}
only and
G_{d}^{q}
is finite, the solution reveals the remarkable structure for the form factor, namely, at given order
a_{s}^{i}
, all the poles except
the single pole in
\varepsilon
can be predicted from knowledge of
K_{d}^{q}
and
G_{d}^{q}
at previous orders up to
a_{s}^{i-1}
.
Explicit results for
K_{d}^{q}
and
G_{d}^{q}
are presented in
Moch and Vogt 2005
;
Ravindran 2006a
and also in App.
A
.

Next, we briefly review the structure of the AP kernels in eq. (
20
) and the associated splitting functions, controlling their evolution as a function of the factorisation scale
\mu_{F}
.
We denote them collectively by
\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathds{L}$}}_{q\leftarrow q}
and
\mathds{P}_{qq}
for (polarized) space-like and time-like evolution, i.e.,
(\Delta)\Gamma_{q\leftarrow q}
and
\tilde{\Gamma}_{q\leftarrow q}
.
In the threshold limit only the diagonal slitting functions
\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c},c=q,\overline{q}
contribute,

\displaystyle\mu_{F}^{2}\frac{d}{d\mu_{F}^{2}}\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathds{L}$}}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2},\xi)=\frac{1}{2}\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2},\xi)\otimes\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathds{L}$}}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2},\xi)\,,

(28)

where
\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
.
The splitting functions are expanded in powers of
a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2})
as
\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{F}^{2})\mathds{P}^{c}_{i-1}
and
we have suppressed any dependence on
\varepsilon
in both
{\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathds{L}$}}_{c\leftarrow c}}
and
\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c}
.
Due to its simple structure the all order solution of eq. (
28
) takes the simple form

\displaystyle\reflectbox{\rotatebox[origin]{180.0}{$\mathds{L}$}}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2},\xi)={\cal C}\exp\left({1\over 2}\int_{0}^{\mu_{F}^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c}(\lambda^{2},\xi)\right)\,,

(29)

where the symbol
{\cal C}

denotes an ordered exponential, appropriately defined through iterated convolutions with respect to
x^{\prime}
or
z^{\prime}
, see
Ravindran 2006a
.
The relevant splitting functions are known to third order in perturbative QCD
Moch et al. 2004
;
Vogt et al. 2004
;
Moch et al. 2014
;
Almasy et al. 2012
;
Chen et al. 2021
and their expansion in the soft and collinear limit reads

\displaystyle\mathds{P}_{c\leftarrow c}(\mu_{F}^{2},\xi)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\Bigg(\frac{A^{c}(\mu_{F}^{2})}{(1-\xi)_{+}}+B^{c}(\mu_{F}^{2})\delta(1-\xi)

(30)

\displaystyle+\mathds{C}^{c}(\mu_{F}^{2})\ln(1-\xi)+\mathds{D}^{c}(\mu_{F}^{2})\Bigg)\,,

in terms of the cusp and the virtual anomalous dimensions
A^{c}
and
B^{c}
already encountered in the form factor above.
The NSV terms, denoted as
\mathds{C}^{c}=C^{q},
and
\mathds{D}^{c}=D^{q}
, are determined from
A^{c}
,
B^{c}
and coefficients of the QCD
\beta
-function at lower orders.
There is distinction between space- and time-like kinematics, i.e.
\mathds{C}^{c}=C^{q},\mathds{D}^{c}=D^{q}
for space-like and
\mathds{C}^{c}=\tilde{C}^{q},\mathds{D}^{c}=\tilde{D}^{q}
for time-like splitting functions.
Explicit results are isted in App.
G
.

The final ingredient in the threshold factorization formulae in eq. (
20
) is the soft function
\hat{S}_{q}
.
A differential equation for
\hat{S}_{q}
in dimensional regularisation, organizing its all-order perturbative structure, can be obtained with eq. (
20
) and the differential equation for the form factor, see eq. (
25
).

\displaystyle Q^{2}{d\hat{\cal S}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\over dQ^{2}}=\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\otimes\hat{\cal S}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\,,

(31)

where we have suppressed the dependence on
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
and

\displaystyle\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)\!\!

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\!\!Q^{2}{d\over dQ^{2}}\Big({\cal C}\ln(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}(Q^{2},\mu_{R}^{2},\mu_{F}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon))

(32)

\displaystyle-\ln|\hat{F}_{q}(Q^{2},\varepsilon)|^{2}\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})\Big)\,.

The fact that
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
is renormalization group invariant with respect to
\mu_{R}
and
\mu_{F}
implies that the
Q^{2}
derivative of
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}
in the first term in eq. (
32
) has to be a function of only
Q^{2}
and
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
. This implies that
\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
is independent of
\mu_{R}
and
\mu_{F}
.
Observe that the first term is finite and the second term contains both singular and finite parts through
K_{d}^{q}
and
G_{d}^{q}
via
K+G
equation of
\Gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}
, see eq. (
25
).
This means that we can express the kernel
\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
in eq. (
32
) as a sum of singular term
\overline{K}_{d}^{q}
and finite
\overline{G}_{d}^{q}
pieces to all orders in perturbation theory, similar to the
Q^{2}
evolution equation of the form factor, i.e.,
\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}=(\overline{K}_{d}^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})+\overline{G}_{d}^{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime}))/2
.
Since the singular part of the kernel comes from
K_{d}^{q}
of the form factor, the term
\overline{K}_{d}^{q}
can depend only on
\mu_{s}
and process independent anomalous dimension,
A^{q}
.
The finite
\overline{G}_{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},z)
will contain
G_{d}^{q}
from the form factor and the other process dependent terms from CF
(\Delta)C_{J,qq}
.

The fact that the kernel admits the decomposition of singular and finite parts as it happens for the form factor, implies that the
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
is also factorisable, i.e., we can write
\hat{\cal S}_{q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})=Z_{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\otimes{\cal S}_{q,{\rm fin}}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
, where
{\cal S}_{q,{\rm fin}}
is IR finite,
Z_{q}
is IR singular and identify the IR singular
\overline{K}_{d}^{q}=2d\ln Z_{q}/d\ln(Q^{2})
and IR finite
\overline{G}_{d}^{q}=2d\ln{\cal S}_{q,fin}/d\ln(Q^{2})
.
Since
\overline{K}_{d}^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})
is independent of
Q^{2}
, we can fix only the
\ln(Q^{2})
terms in
Z_{q}
. The factorisation of
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
in terms of
Z_{q}
and
\hat{\cal S}_{q,\text{fin}}
at arbitrary scale
\mu_{s}
leads to the renormalisation group equation,

\displaystyle\mu_{s}^{2}{dZ_{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2})\over d\mu_{s}^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2})\otimes Z_{q}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2})\,,

where we have suppressed any dependence on
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
in
Z_{q}
and
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
.
From our two-loop results we find that
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
takes the remarkable structure, namely
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}(Q^{2},\mu_{s}^{2})=\xi_{1}(\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\ln(Q^{2}/\mu_{s}^{2})+\xi_{2}(\mu_{s}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
.
This is expected to continue to all orders in perturbation theory because it results from the IR pole structure of
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
.
In other words, the singular part of
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
, namely,
Z_{q}
has to contain precisley those IR poles to cancel against the ones from the form factor and AP kernels leaving
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}
finite and this pole structure determines the structure of
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
.
Since the poles of the form factor and AP kernels are controlled by the cusp, virtual and eikonal anomalous dimensions,
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}\!\!

\displaystyle=\!\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})A^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\ln\Big({Q^{2}\over\mu_{s}^{2}}\Big)+P^{\prime}_{q\leftarrow q}(\mu_{s},\overline{x}^{\prime},\overline{z}^{\prime})\,,

(33)

where

\displaystyle P^{\prime}_{q\leftarrow q}=

\displaystyle\bigg[-{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime}){f^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})}+\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime}){A^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\over(\bar{x}^{\prime})_{+}}

\displaystyle+\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})\Big(C^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\ln(\bar{x}^{\prime})+D^{q}(\mu_{s}^{2})\Big)\bigg]

\displaystyle+\Big(\overline{x}^{\prime}\leftrightarrow\overline{z}^{\prime},C^{q}\leftrightarrow\tilde{C}^{q},D^{q}\leftrightarrow\tilde{D}^{q}\Big)\,,

(34)

and we use the shorthand notation
\bar{x}^{\prime}=1-x^{\prime},\bar{z}^{\prime}=1-z^{\prime}
.

With the boundary
Q^{2}=0
,
\hat{\cal S}_{q}(Q^{2}=0,x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})
to all orders in
a_{s}
we obtain the solution to eq. (
31
) as

\displaystyle\hat{\cal S}_{q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal C}\exp\left(\int_{0}^{Q^{2}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}\Gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}(\lambda^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)\right)

(35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal C}\exp\left(2\Phi_{d,q}(Q^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)\right)\,.

The exponent
\Phi_{d,q}
can be decomposed into an IR singular part
\Phi_{d,q,s}
and a finite part
\Phi_{d,q,f}
, i.e.,
\Phi_{d,q}=\Phi_{d,q,s}+\Phi_{d,q,f}
.
Using this decomposition, we obtain
Z_{q}={\cal C}\exp(2\Phi_{d,q,s})
and
{\cal S}_{q,fin}={\cal C}\exp(2\Phi_{d,q,f})
.
The singular part is controlled by
\gamma_{\hat{\cal S}_{q}}
given in eq. (
II
). A careful inspection of the one- and two-loop results
for
\hat{\cal S}_{q}
, i.e., using the results presented in
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
, provides hints to its all order structure.
However, before we proceed, we split
\Phi_{d,q}
into the sum of two parts.
The SV part denoted by
\Phi_{d,q}^{\text{SV}}
contains only distributions such as
\delta(1-\xi)
and
{\cal D}_{\xi,i}
, while NSV part
\Phi_{d,q}^{\text{NSV}}
summarizes all terms that are products of distributions and
\ln^{i}(1-\xi)
with
\xi=x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
.
We find that
\Phi_{d,q}^{\text{SV}}
is symmetric under the interchange of
x^{\prime}
and
z^{\prime}
while
\Phi_{d,q}^{\text{NSV}}
is not.
Our observations for
\Phi_{d,q}
read as follows:

•

For the SV terms, at order
\hat{a}_{s}
, the leading pole in
\varepsilon
starts at order two, at
\hat{a}_{s}^{2}
it is three and it is expected to increase by one unit at every order in
\hat{a}_{s}
.
Hence, at order
\hat{a}_{s}^{i}
, the leading pole in
\varepsilon

will be of order
i+1
. As we had already mentioned, this pole structure is completely dictated by pole structure of
\ln|\hat{F}_{q}|^{2}
and the AP kernels. For the NSV terms, we find that at order
\hat{a}_{s}
the leading pole in
\varepsilon
is of order one and at two loops, it is two etc. This pattern indicates that the increment of one unit for the leading poles is expected to continue
with the order of perturbation and hence at order
\hat{a}_{s}^{i}
, the leading pole
will be of order
i
.

•

At every order in
\hat{a}_{s}
, for a given color factor, the combination of
\varepsilon

and the leading logarithm shows uniform transcendentality weight. If we
assign
n_{\varepsilon}
weight for
\varepsilon^{-n}
and
n_{{\cal D}}
for
{\cal D}_{\overline{\xi}}^{n}
,
n_{L}
for

\ln^{n}(1-\xi)
and
n_{\zeta}
for
\zeta_{n}
then the highest weight at
every order in
\varepsilon

shows uniform transcendentality
\omega=n_{\varepsilon}+n_{{\cal D}}+n_{L}+n_{\zeta}
. For example, in SV, at one loop, we find
\omega=2
at
every order of
\varepsilon
and at two loops it is three (
\omega=3
) etc. For NSV,
\omega=1
at one loop and
\omega=2
at two loops at every order in

\varepsilon
etc.

The above observations on the fixed order results and the resemblance of eq. (
25
) with eq. (
31
), the similarity in the pole structure of the respective kernels eqs. (
26
) and (
32
) and the renormalization group invariance reveal an all order structure for
\phi^{\text{SV},(i)}_{d,q}
of the form:

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Phi}^{\text{SV}}_{q}=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\over\mu^{2}\right)^{i{\varepsilon\over 2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}\Bigg[{(i\varepsilon)^{2}\over 4(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})}\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(i)}(\varepsilon)\Bigg]\,,

(36)

where

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(i)}(\varepsilon)=\frac{1}{i\epsilon}\Big[\overline{K}_{d}^{q(i)}(\varepsilon)+\overline{G}_{d,\text{SV}}^{q(i)}(\varepsilon)\Big]\,,

(37)

and for NSV part

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Phi}^{\text{NSV}}_{q}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\over\mu^{2}\right)^{i{\varepsilon\over 2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}\Bigg[{i\varepsilon\over 4(1-x^{\prime})}\hat{\varphi}_{d,z^{\prime},q}^{\text{NSV},(i)}(z^{\prime},\varepsilon)+{i\varepsilon\over 4(1-z^{\prime})}\hat{\varphi}_{d,x^{\prime},q}^{\text{NSV},(i)}(x^{\prime},\varepsilon)\Bigg]\,.

(38)

For reader’s convenience, we have listed the results of
\overline{K}_{d}^{q(i)}
and
\overline{G}_{d,\text{SV}}^{q(i)}
in the App.
B
.
After substituting them the constants
\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(i)}(\varepsilon)
are found to be

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}^{\text{SV},(1)}_{d,q}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg(2A_{1}^{q}\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg({\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(2)}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg(-\beta_{0}A_{1}^{q}\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg({1\over 2}A_{2}^{q}-\beta_{0}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over 2\varepsilon}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(3)}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{4}}\Bigg({8\over 9}\beta_{0}^{2}A_{1}^{q}\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg(-{2\over 9}\beta_{1}A_{1}^{q}-{8\over 9}\beta_{0}A_{2}^{q}-{4\over 3}\beta_{0}^{2}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg({2\over 9}A_{3}^{c}-{1\over 3}\beta_{1}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)-{4\over 3}\beta_{0}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)

\displaystyle+{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg({1\over 3}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,3}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\hat{\phi}_{d,q}^{\text{SV},(4)}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{5}}\Bigg(-\beta_{0}^{3}A_{1}^{q}\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{4}}\Bigg({2\over 3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}A_{1}^{q}+{3\over 2}\beta_{0}^{2}A_{2}^{q}-2\beta_{0}^{3}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)-{1\over\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg({1\over 12}\beta_{2}A_{1}^{q}-{1\over 4}\beta_{1}A_{2}^{q}-{3\over 4}\beta_{0}A_{3}^{q}

\displaystyle+{4\over 3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)+3\beta_{0}^{2}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg({1\over 8}A_{4}^{q}-{1\over 6}\beta_{2}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)-{1\over 2}\beta_{1}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)-{3\over 2}\beta_{0}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,3}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg({1\over 4}{\cal\overline{G}}_{d,4}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)\,.

The cusp anomalous dimensions
A^{q}_{i}
in the above equations carry the right sign to cancel all the leading singularities against the form factor contributions in CFs.
The
\varepsilon
dependent terms
\overline{\cal G}^{q}_{d,i}
originate from
|{\hat{F}}_{q}|^{2}
as well as the CFs
(\Delta)\mathcal{C}_{J,qq}
in eq. (
32
).
Using renormalization group invariance and demanding the cancellation of single poles in eq. (
II
) against the form factor and the AP kernels, we can parameterise
{\overline{\cal G}}^{q}_{d,i}
as,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q}_{d,i}(\varepsilon)=-f_{i}^{q}+\overline{\chi}_{d,i}^{q}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{j}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(j)}_{d,i}\,,

(40)

where

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}^{q}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=0\,,\overline{\chi}^{q}_{d,2}=-2\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}\,,\overline{\chi}^{q}_{d,3}=-2\beta_{1}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}-2\beta_{0}\left(\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}+2\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}\right)

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}^{q}_{d,4}

\displaystyle=-2\beta_{2}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}-2\beta_{1}\left(\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}+4\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}\right)-2\beta_{0}\left(\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,3}+2\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,2}+4\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\cal G}^{q,(3)}_{d,1}\right)\,.

(41)

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{-\zeta_{2}\Bigg\}\,,\qquad\qquad\overline{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}=C_{F}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{3}\zeta_{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(3)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{80}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,\qquad\qquad\overline{\cal G}^{q,(4)}_{d,1}=C_{F}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{20}\zeta_{5}-\frac{1}{24}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(5)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{144}\zeta_{3}^{2}-\frac{1}{896}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{-\frac{328}{81}+\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{10}{3}\zeta_{2}\Bigg\}+C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{2428}{81}-\frac{44}{3}\zeta_{3}-\frac{67}{3}\zeta_{2}-4\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{976}{243}-\frac{70}{27}\zeta_{3}-\frac{28}{9}\zeta_{2}+\frac{29}{60}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{7288}{243}-43\zeta_{5}-\frac{469}{27}\zeta_{3}-\frac{202}{9}\zeta_{2}+\frac{71}{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{319}{120}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,3}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{11584}{2187}-\frac{320}{81}\zeta_{3}-\frac{316}{27}\zeta_{2}+\frac{152}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{42727}{324}-\frac{112}{3}\zeta_{5}-\frac{1672}{27}\zeta_{3}-\frac{275}{6}\zeta_{2}+40\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{152}{15}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{7135981}{8748}-\frac{1430}{3}\zeta_{5}-936\zeta_{3}+\frac{536}{3}\zeta_{3}^{2}-\frac{379417}{486}\zeta_{2}+\frac{4136}{9}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{1538}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{17392}{315}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{716509}{4374}-\frac{148}{3}\zeta_{5}-\frac{12356}{81}\zeta_{3}-\frac{51053}{243}\zeta_{2}+\frac{392}{9}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{1372}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,.

(42)

Here,
C_{A}=N_{c}
and
C_{F}\equiv\frac{N_{c}^{2}-1}{2N_{c}}
are the quadradic Casimirs of the adjoint and fundamental representations of
SU(N_{c})
, respectively,
while
n_{f}
denoted the number of quark flavors.
Note, that the constants in eq. (
II
) are also related to the threshold exponent
\textbf{D}^{q}\big(a_{s}(q^{2}(1-z)^{2})\big)
via eq. (46) of
Ravindran 2006b
.

The results for
\overline{\cal G}^{q,(j)}_{d,i}
for
i=1,2
have been obtained from the NLO and NNLO CFs expanded to sufficient order in
\varepsilon

Altarelli et al. 1979
;
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024a
.
We find that these results are identical to the corresponding ones for the rapidity distribution of pairs of leptons in DY production
Ravindran et al. 2007
;
Ravindran and Smith 2007
;
Ahmed et al. 2014a
;
Ahmed et al. 2015
;
Banerjee et al. 2018b
;
Banerjee et al. 2018a
;
A H et al. 2021
;
Ahmed et al. 2021
;
A H et al. 2022
;
Ravindran et al. 2023
;
Ravindran et al. 2024
, see also
Anderle et al. 2013a
;
Westmark and Owens 2017
;
Banerjee et al. 2018a
,
where a conjecture on the relation between these two processes in the threshold limit had been proposed.
Assuming that this conjecture holds true beyond NNLO, we have used
\overline{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,3}
of the rapidity distribution for the DY process also for SIDIS at N
3
LO.

The all order result given in eq. (
36
) is useful to obtain predictions for the SV part of the CFs order by order
a_{s}
.
However, to study the numerical impact of the all order result, we need to perform a resummation in Mellin space.
This can be achieved by expressing the summation in the integral form. The integral representation for
\mathrm{\Phi}_{q}^{\text{SV}}
for the DY process is given in
Ravindran et al. 2007
and the same can be
used for SIDIS in eq. (
36
):

\displaystyle\Phi^{\text{SV}}_{q}(\hat{a}_{s},Q^{2},\mu^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})\Bigg({1\over\bar{x}^{\prime}}\Bigg\{\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}A^{q}\left(a_{s}(\lambda^{2})\right)+\overline{G}^{q}_{d}\left(a_{s}\left(Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}\right),\varepsilon\right)\Bigg\}\Bigg)_{+}

(43)

\displaystyle+Q^{2}{d\over dQ^{2}}\Bigg[\Bigg({1\over 4\bar{x}^{\prime}\bar{z}^{\prime}}\Bigg\{\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}\bar{z}^{\prime}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}A^{q}\left(a_{s}(\lambda^{2})\right)+\overline{G}^{q}_{d}\left(a_{s}\left(Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}\bar{z}^{\prime}\right),\varepsilon\right)\Bigg\}\Bigg)_{+}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left({Q^{2}\over\mu^{2}}\right)^{i{\varepsilon\over 2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}~\hat{\phi}^{\text{SV},(i)}_{d,q}(\varepsilon)+{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{z}^{\prime})\left({1\over\bar{x}^{\prime}}\right)_{+}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left({\mu_{F}^{2}\over\mu^{2}}\right)^{i{\varepsilon\over 2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}~\overline{K}^{q,(i)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+(x^{\prime}\leftrightarrow z^{\prime})\,,

In complete analogy to the SV part, using the renormalization group, the coefficients
\varphi^{\text{NSV},(i)}_{d,\xi,q}
in the NSV solution
\Phi^{\text{NSV}}_{q}
, eq. (
38
) can be expanded as follows,

\displaystyle\hat{\varphi}_{d,\xi,q}^{\text{NSV},(1)}(\xi,\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\,,

\displaystyle{\hat{\varphi}_{d,\xi,q}^{\text{NSV},(2)}(\xi,\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}(-\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon))+\frac{1}{2\varepsilon}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\,,

\displaystyle{\hat{\varphi}_{d,\xi,q}^{\text{NSV},(3)}(\xi,\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\bigg(\frac{4}{3}\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\bigg)+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\bigg(-\frac{1}{3}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)-\frac{4}{3}\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\bigg)+\frac{1}{3\varepsilon}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,3}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\,,

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\hat{\varphi}_{d,\xi,q}^{\text{NSV},(4)}(\xi,\varepsilon)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}(-2\beta_{0}^{3}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon))+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\bigg(\frac{4}{3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)+3\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\bigg(-\frac{1}{6}\beta_{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)-\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)-\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,3}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\bigg)+\frac{1}{4\varepsilon}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,4}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)\,.

(44)

In order to cancel the collinear poles from the AP kernels, the quantities
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,i}^{q}(\xi,\varepsilon)
can be further decomposed as

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,x^{\prime},i}^{q}(x^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2L^{q}_{i}(x^{\prime})+\overline{\chi}_{d,x^{\prime},i}^{q}(x^{\prime})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{j}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,x^{\prime},i}^{q,(j)}(x^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,z^{\prime},i}^{q}(z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\tilde{L}^{q}_{i}(z^{\prime})+\overline{\chi}_{d,z^{\prime},i}^{q}(z^{\prime})+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{j}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,z^{\prime},i}^{q,(j)}(z^{\prime})

with
L_{i}^{q}(x^{\prime})=C_{i}^{q}\ln(1-x^{\prime})+D_{i}^{q}
and
\tilde{L}_{i}^{q}(z^{\prime})=\tilde{C}_{i}^{q}\ln(1-z^{\prime})+\tilde{D}_{i}^{q}
and

\displaystyle\overline{\chi}_{d,\xi,i}^{q}(\xi)=\overline{\chi}_{d,i}^{q}\Big|_{\left(\overline{\cal G}^{q,(j)}_{d,i}\rightarrow\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,i}^{q,(j)}(\xi)\right)}\,,

(46)

where
\overline{\chi}^{q}_{d,i}
is given in eq. (
II
).
The coefficients
C^{q}_{i}(\tilde{C}^{q}_{i})
and
D^{q}_{i}(\tilde{D}^{q}_{i})
are related to the cusp
A^{q}_{i}
and virtual
B^{q}_{i}
anomalous dimensions in the following way
Moch et al. 2004
;
Dokshitzer et al. 2006
.
For space-like splitting functions one has

\displaystyle D^{q}_{1}=

\displaystyle-A_{1}^{q}\,,\qquad D_{2}^{q}=-A_{2}^{q}+A_{1}^{q}\left(B_{1}^{q}-\beta_{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle D_{3}^{q}=

\displaystyle-A_{3}^{q}-A_{1}^{q}\left(-B_{2}^{q}+\beta_{1}\right)-A_{2}^{q}\left(-B_{1}^{q}+\beta_{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle C_{1}^{q}=

\displaystyle 0\,,\qquad C_{2}^{q}=\Big(A_{1}^{q}\Big)^{2}\,,\qquad C_{3}^{q}=2A_{1}^{q}A_{2}^{q}\,,

(47)

and for the time-like ones

\displaystyle\tilde{D}^{q}_{1}=

\displaystyle-A_{1}^{q}\,,\qquad\tilde{D}_{2}^{q}=-A_{2}^{q}+A_{1}^{q}\left(-B_{1}^{q}-\beta_{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{D}_{3}^{q}=

\displaystyle-A_{3}^{q}-A_{1}^{q}\left(B_{2}^{q}+\beta_{1}\right)-A_{2}^{q}\left(B_{1}^{q}+\beta_{0}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{C}_{1}^{q}=

\displaystyle 0\,,\qquad\tilde{C}_{2}^{q}=-\Big(A_{1}^{q}\Big)^{2}\,,\qquad\tilde{C}_{3}^{q}=-2A_{1}^{q}A_{2}^{q}\,.

(48)

The renormalized coefficients
\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,i}^{q,(j)}(\xi)
in the above equations are parametrised in terms of

\ln^{k}(1-\xi),k=0,1,\cdots
and all the terms vanishing as
\xi\rightarrow 1
are dropped

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,i}^{q,(j)}(\xi)=\sum_{k=0}^{i+j-1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,i}^{q,(j,k)}\ln^{k}(1-\xi)\,.

(49)

The leading term in
\ln(1-\xi)
at every order in
a_{s}
depends on the power of
a_{s}
, but also the power of
\varepsilon
.
The leading power of
\ln(1-\xi)
can be determined by expanding the partonic cross sections to high accuracy in
\varepsilon
at every order in
a_{s}
.
One can also use the mass factorised results such as the CFs to determine them.
In the former approach, we havev computed the partonic cross section at NLO to an accuracy of
\varepsilon
up to third order and at NNLO to
zeroth power in
\varepsilon
.
We expect that the observed pattern for those logarithms persists at higher orders and we can extrapolate to all orders in
\hat{a}_{s}
and
\varepsilon
by fixing the highest power for
\ln(1-\xi)
to be
i+j-1
in eq. (
49
).

For a resummation to all orders an integral form for
\Phi^{\rm{NSV}}_{q}
is needed to study its numerical impact.
In order to do this, we decompose
\Phi^{\rm{NSV}}_{q}
as a sum of a singular part and a finite part.
The singular part can be obtained by setting all the terms to zero in eq. (
II
) except the first one,
2L_{i}^{q}
and
2\tilde{L}_{i}^{q}
.
It contains the correct terms that will cancel against NSV terms of the AP kernels.
We write
\Phi^{{\text{NSV}}}_{q}=\Phi^{{\text{NSV}}}_{q,s}+\Phi^{{\text{NSV}}}_{q,fin}
, where the first term is singular as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
and contains only
L_{i}^{q}
as well as
\tilde{L}_{i}^{q}
and the second one contains the finite remainder.
This decomposition facilitates to express
\Phi^{\rm{NSV}}_{q}
in an integral form as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Phi}_{q}^{\text{NSV}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}\bar{z}^{\prime}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\tilde{L}^{q}\big(a_{s}(\lambda^{2}),z^{\prime}\big)+{1\over 2}\Bigg({1\over\bar{x}^{\prime}}\tilde{L}^{q}\big(a_{s}(Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}\bar{z}^{\prime}),z^{\prime}\big)\Bigg)_{+}+{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\varphi_{d,z^{\prime},f}^{q}\big(a_{s}(Q^{2}\bar{z}^{\prime}),z^{\prime}\big)

(50)

\displaystyle+{1\over 2}\Bigg({1\over\bar{x}^{\prime}}Q^{2}{d\over dQ^{2}}\varphi_{d,z^{\prime},f}^{q}\big(a_{s}(Q^{2}\bar{x}^{\prime}\bar{z}^{\prime}),z^{\prime}\big)\Bigg)_{+}+{1\over 2}\delta(\bar{x}^{\prime})\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left({\mu_{F}^{2}\over\mu^{2}}\right)^{i{\varepsilon\over 2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}\varphi^{(i)}_{d,z^{\prime},s}(z^{\prime},\varepsilon)

\displaystyle+\left(\tilde{L}^{q}\leftrightarrow L^{q},x^{\prime}\leftrightarrow z^{\prime}\right)\,.

In the above equation, the term containg
\varphi_{d,\xi,s}^{(i)}
is singular while the remaining ones are all finite as
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.
The fact that
\mathrm{\Phi}_{q}^{\text{NSV}}
is renormalization group invariant implies that
\varphi^{q}_{d,\xi,f}
satisfies the equations

\displaystyle\mu_{F}^{2}{d\over d\mu_{F}^{2}}\varphi^{q}_{d,x^{\prime},f}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L^{q}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),x^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle\mu_{F}^{2}{d\over d\mu_{F}^{2}}\varphi^{q}_{d,z^{\prime},f}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{q}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),z^{\prime})\,,

(51)

where

\displaystyle L^{q}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),x^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{F}^{2})L_{i}^{q}(x^{\prime})\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{L}^{q}(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2}),z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{F}^{2})\tilde{L}_{i}^{q}(z^{\prime})\,.

(52)

Having fixed the divergent part of

\mathrm{\Phi}_{q}^{\rm{NSV}}
completely, we need to understand all order structure of the finite piece
\varphi^{q}_{d,\xi,f}
.
Using eq. (
49
), we can expand this functions in powers of renormalised coupling
a_{s}
:

\displaystyle\varphi_{d,\xi,f}^{q}(a_{s}(\lambda^{2}),\xi)=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\lambda^{2})\sum_{k=0}^{i}\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,i}^{(k)}\ln^{k}(1-\xi)\,,

(53)

where the highest power of
\ln(1-\xi)
aligns with that in eq. (
49
).
The coefficients
\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,i}^{(k)}
can be expressed in terms of their unrenormalised counter part
\overline{\cal G}^{q,(j,k)}_{d,\xi,i}
’s in eq. (
49
) as:

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,1}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(1,k)},\quad\quad k=0,1

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,2}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q,(1,k)}+\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(2,k)}\bigg),\quad\quad k=0,1,2

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,3}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{3}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,3}^{q,(1,k)}+\frac{2}{3}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(2,k)}+\frac{2}{3}\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q,(2,k)}+\frac{4}{3}\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(3,k)}\bigg),\quad\quad k=0,1,2,3

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,4}^{(k)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{1}{4}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,4}^{q,(1,k)}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(2,k)}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q,(2,k)}+\frac{1}{2}\beta_{0}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,3}^{q,(2,k)}+2\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(3,k)}+\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,2}^{q,(3,k)}+2\beta_{0}^{3}\overline{\mathcal{G}}_{d,\xi,1}^{q,(4,k)}\bigg),

(54)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\hskip 207.0021ptk=0,1,2,3,4

The structure of divergent and finite pieces of

{\rm\Phi}_{q}^{\text{NSV}}
allows us to determine the coefficients
\overline{\cal G}^{q,(j,k)}_{d,\xi,i}
.
Since our final result depends on

\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,i}^{(k)}
we can directly determine them by comparison with the fixed order results for the CFs, known to NNLO level
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
and they are listed below:

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(0)}_{q,x^{\prime},1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\bigg\{-4\bigg\}\,,\quad\overline{\varphi}^{(1)}_{q,x^{\prime},1}=0\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(0)}_{q,x^{\prime},2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{1108}{27}-28\zeta_{3}-\frac{44}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{52}{27}-\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{8\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(1)}_{q,x^{\prime},2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{-10\bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{10\bigg\}\,,\quad\overline{\varphi}^{(2)}_{q,x^{\prime},2}=C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\,,

(55)

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(0)}_{q,z^{\prime},1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\,,\quad\overline{\varphi}^{(1)}_{q,z^{\prime},1}=0\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(0)}_{q,z^{\prime},2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{1492}{27}-28\zeta_{3}-\frac{68}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{364}{27}-\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{16\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{\varphi}^{(1)}_{q,z^{\prime},2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{10\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{10\bigg\}\,,\quad\overline{\varphi}^{(2)}_{q,z^{\prime},2}=C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{-4\bigg\}\,,

(56)

Note that the constants
\overline{\varphi}^{(k)}_{q,x^{\prime},i}
are different from
\overline{\varphi}^{(k)}_{q,z^{\prime},i}
unlike those that appearing in rapidity distributions of the DY process, see
A H et al. 2021
.

Having obtained the general structure
of the soft function, the final step is the substitution of the integral representations for

\Phi_{q}^{\rm{SV}}
in eq. (
43
),
for
\Phi_{q}^{\rm{NSV}}
in eq. (
50
),
the solutions to the form factor in eq. (
27
) and the AP kernels in eq. (
29
)
into eq. (
20
).
We find that all the soft and collinear singularities cancel among themselves leaving SV and NSV parts of the CFs IR finite.

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}^{\rm SV+NSV}=\mathcal{C}\exp\bigg(\Psi^{q}_{d}\big(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon\big)\bigg)\bigg|_{\varepsilon=0}\,,

(57)

where the IR finite expression
\Psi_{d}^{q}
takes a simple form

\displaystyle\mathrm{\Psi}_{d}^{q}=

\displaystyle\frac{\delta(\overline{x}^{\prime})}{2}\Bigg(\Bigg\{\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}\overline{z}^{\prime}}{d\lambda^{2}\over\lambda^{2}}\mathcal{\tilde{P}}^{q}\left(a_{s}(\lambda^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)+\mathcal{Q}^{q}\left(a_{s}(Q_{2}^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)\Bigg)_{+}+\frac{1}{4}\Bigg(\frac{1}{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\Bigg\{\mathcal{\tilde{P}}^{q}\left(a_{s}(Q_{12}^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)+2\tilde{L}^{q}\left(a_{s}(Q_{12}^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)

\displaystyle+Q^{2}{\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}}\bigg(\mathcal{Q}^{q}\left(a_{s}(Q_{2}^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)+2{\varphi}^{q}_{d,z^{\prime},f}\left(a_{s}(Q_{2}^{2}),\overline{z}^{\prime}\right)\bigg)\Bigg\}\Bigg)_{+}+\frac{1}{2}\delta(\overline{x}^{\prime})~\delta(\overline{z}^{\prime})~\text{ln}\Big(g^{q}_{d,0}\left(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2})\right)\Big)+\Big(x^{\prime}\leftrightarrow z^{\prime}\Big)\,.

(58)

Here, we use the short-hands
\overline{x}^{\prime}=(1-x^{\prime})
and
\overline{z}^{\prime}=(1-z^{\prime})
,
Q_{1}^{2}=Q^{2}~\overline{x}^{\prime}
,
Q_{2}^{2}=Q^{2}~\overline{z}^{\prime}
and
Q^{2}_{12}=Q^{2}~\overline{x}^{\prime}~\overline{z}^{\prime}
.
The subscript
+
indicates the standard plus distribution.
In the above equation,
{\cal P}^{q},\tilde{\cal P}^{q}
are related diagonal splitting functions given in eq. (
30
),

\displaystyle{\cal P}^{q}(a_{s},\overline{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=P_{q\leftarrow q}(\mu_{s}^{2},\overline{x}^{\prime})-2B^{q}(a_{s})\delta(\overline{x}^{\prime}),

\displaystyle\tilde{{\cal P}}^{q}(a_{s},\overline{z}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\tilde{P}_{q\leftarrow q}(\mu_{s}^{2},\overline{z}^{\prime})-2B^{q}(a_{s})\delta(\overline{z}^{\prime})\,,

(59)

and
\mathcal{Q}^{q}_{d}(a_{s},\overline{z}^{\prime})
=
\frac{2}{\overline{z}^{\prime}}G^{q}_{d}(a_{s})
+ 2
\varphi^{q}_{d,z^{\prime},f}(a_{s},\overline{z}^{\prime})
and similar for
x^{\prime}
.
Note that
g_{d,0}^{q}
is process-dependent and receives contributions only from the form factor and finite parts of
\mathrm{\Phi}^{\text{SV}}_{q}
.

The result for
(\Delta){\cal C}^{{\text{SV+NSV}}}_{J,qq}
in eq. (
II
) is valid to all orders in
a_{s}
.
The exponent
\Psi^{q}_{d}
contains universal (process independent) quantities such as the QCD
\beta
function and anomalous dimensions
A^{q},B^{q},f^{q}
etc. and process dependent quantities
G^{q}_{d},\varphi^{q}_{d,\xi,f}
and
g^{q}_{d,0}
.
Each of them can be expanded in powers of
a_{s}
, hence
\Psi^{q}_{d}
is expanded as

\displaystyle\Psi^{q}_{d}(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{R}^{2})\Psi^{q,(i)}_{d}(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2},\mu_{R}^{2})\,,

(60)

where we have suppressed the dependence on
x^{\prime},z^{\prime}
in
\Psi^{q}_{d}
and
\Psi^{q,(i)}_{d}
.

The result in eq. (
57
) is the main result of the paper.
It sums up towers of SV and NSV terms to all orders in
a_{s}
in those CFs, which are dominat near threshold.
The particular logarithmic structure of
\Psi^{q}_{d}
given in eq. (
II
)
allows us to predict certain SV and NSV terms to any order in
a_{s}
, i.e.,
a_{s}^{n}
for the diagonal CFs for all
n\geq 1
, provided
\Psi^{q}_{d}
is known up to order
a_{s}^{m}
with
n>m
.
The achievable accuray of those predictions for
\Psi^{q,(i)}_{d}
s for
i=1,2,3
and for general
i=n

is summarized in Tabs.
1
and
2
.
For example, if we know only
\Psi_{d}^{q,(1)}
, we can predict the following terms for
(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,cc}^{(2)}
, i.e., for the SV part,
\{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{3},\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{3}\}
and
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{i}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{j}\}
for
i+j=2
and, likewise, for the NSV one

\{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}{\cal L}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{3},\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}{\cal L}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{3}\}
, and
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{i}{\cal L}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{j},{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{i}{\cal L}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{j}\}
for
i+j=2
.
If, in addition, we allow for terms present in
\Psi_{d}^{q,(2)}
which depend only
on information about
\Psi_{d}^{q,(1)}
, then, because of the renormalization group equations, we can predict,
for the SV part also
\{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{2},\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{2}\}
and
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{i}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{j}\}
for
i+j=1
,
and for the NSV part
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{1}{\cal L}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{0},{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{1}{\cal L}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{0}\}
.
Knowledge on higher-order corrections to the anomalous dimensions leads to predictions for the SV terms
\{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{i},\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{i}\}
for
i=0,1
and
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{0}{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{0}\}
and for the NSV terms
\{{\cal D}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{0}{\cal L}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{i},{\cal D}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}^{0}{\cal L}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}^{i}\}
for
i=0,1
.
We elaborate on this further below when we make predictions at third and fourth order in
a_{s}
using the
\Psi^{q,(i)}_{d}
s at
i=1,2
and partial results on
\Psi^{q,(i)}_{d}
s for
i>2
.

III
Results of the SV and NSV Coefficient functions

In this section, we present the analytic results of the SV and NSV CFs,
(\Delta){\cal C}^{\text{SV}}_{J,cc}
and
(\Delta){\cal C}^{\text{NSV}}_{J,cc}
for
c=\{q,\overline{q}\}
respectively.
We present complete results at NLO and NNLO and partial results up to N
4
LO in the thresold region for the CFs, expanded in
a_{s}
as

(\Delta){\cal C}^{\text{(N)SV}}_{J,cc}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{R}^{2})(\Delta){\cal C}^{(i),\text{(N)SV}}_{J,cc}
.
Their normalization at LO reads
(\Delta){\cal C}^{(0),\text{SV}}_{J,cc}=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})
and
(\Delta){\cal C}^{(0),\text{NSV}}_{J,cc}=0
, and we have set
\mu_{F}^{2}=\mu_{R}^{2}=Q^{2}
in the sequel.

III.0.1
SV Coefficient functions

For the perturbative expansion of the SV part of the CFs,
we define in addition to
C_{A}
and
C_{F}
further group invariants of the color
SU(N_{c})
gauge group, namely
We also define

\frac{d^{abcd}_{F}d^{abcd}_{F}}{N_{c}}=\frac{(N_{c}^{2}-1)(N_{c}^{4}-6N_{c}^{2}+18)}{96N_{c}^{3}}\,,\qquad\frac{d^{abcd}_{F}d^{abcd}_{A}}{N_{c}}=\frac{(N_{c}^{2}-1)(N_{c}^{2}+6)}{48}\,,\qquad N_{4}=\frac{(N_{c}^{2}-4)}{N_{c}}\,.

(61)

Also, below we will use
n_{fv}
, which is defined as the charge weighted sum of quark flavors.
At NLO, i.e. at
{\mathcal{O}}(a_{s})
,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}^{(1),\text{SV}}_{J,cc}

\displaystyle=\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{8\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{2\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{({x}^{\prime}\leftrightarrow{z}^{\prime})}\,.

(62)

The NNLO CF at SV accuracy read

\displaystyle(\Delta)\mathcal{C}^{(2),\text{SV}}_{J,cc}

\displaystyle=\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{3}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{8\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{2}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\Bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}\bigg]+\bm{\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{24\bigg\}\bigg]+\bm{\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{2}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{24\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\bm{\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{44}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{8}{3}\bigg\}\bigg]+\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{1}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{268}{9}-8\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{40}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{64+32\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{1535}{24}-\frac{86}{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{269}{9}\zeta_{2}-\frac{34}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{511}{8}-30\zeta_{3}+29\zeta_{2}-\frac{28}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{127}{12}+\frac{4}{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{38}{9}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]+\bm{\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{134}{9}-4\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{20}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{32+16\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\bigg]

\displaystyle-\bm{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{0}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}}\bigg[C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{808}{27}-28\zeta_{3}-\frac{44}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{112}{27}-\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{32\zeta_{3}\bigg\}\bigg]+\bm{(x^{\prime}\leftrightarrow z^{\prime})}\,.

(63)

a_{s}

the knowledge of

and

At third order, only partial result for NSV contributions can be obtained with the the presently known ingredients, unlike the SV case.
Using the complete second order results for
\Psi_{d}^{q,(1)}
and
\Psi_{d}^{q,(2)}

.

\{\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\}
for

\overline{\varphi}_{q,\xi,3}^{(i)}
for

.
