IV
Resummation

The formalism for the SV and NSV terms applied in the previous section is useful for obtaining results in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space at a given order
a_{s}^{n}
, provided
that the exponent
\Psi^{q}_{d}
is known to order
a_{s}^{m}
, where
m=1,\cdots,n-1
.
It is impractical, though, for studying the numerical impact at all orders in
a_{s}
, because it involves iterated convolutions among distributions as well as among distributions and powers of
\ln(1-\xi)
terms, which become difficult to evaluate at higher orders in
a_{s}
.
All-order resummation of our result in eq. (
57
) is therefore tcarried out in Mellin space spanned by two complex numbers
N_{i},i=1,2
, taking double Mellin moments with respect to
N_{i},i=1,2
of CF given in eq. (
57
).
In Mellin space, the convolution
{\cal C}
of the exponential in eq. (
57
) becomes an exponential of the double Mellin moment of the exponent
\Psi^{q}_{d}
, i.e.,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}^{\vec{N}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\cal N}\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}{x^{\prime}}^{N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}{z^{\prime}}^{N_{2}-1}(\Delta){\cal C}^{\rm{SV+NSV}}_{qq}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

(70)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left({\cal N}\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}{x^{\prime}}^{N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}{z^{\prime}}^{N_{2}-1}\Psi^{q}_{d}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\Big(G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}\Big)\,,

where we have suppressed the dependence on the scales
Q^{2}
and
\mu_{F}^{2}
.
In Mellin
\vec{N}
-space,
\vec{N}=(N_{1},N_{2})
,
the threshold limits of the
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space , namely
\{x^{\prime},z^{\prime}\}\rightarrow\{1,1\}
,
correspond to taking both
\{N_{1},N_{2}\}\rightarrow\{\infty,\infty\}
and
the limits on
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are applied simultaneously.
The symbol
{\cal N}
in eq. (
70
) indicates that we retain only the
(N_{1},N_{2})
space versions of SV and NSV terms, which are denoted as
\overline{\rm{SV}}
and
\overline{\rm{NSV}}
, respectively.
Specifically,
\overline{\rm{SV}}
terms are of the form
\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})

with
i,j=1,\cdots,\infty
.

\overline{\rm{NSV}}
terms, on the other hand, include corrections of the type
1/N_{i}
.
They are of the form

\ln^{i}(N_{1})/N_{1}\ln^{j}(N_{2})
where
i=0,\cdots,\infty,j=1,\cdots,\infty
or of the form

\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})/N_{2}
where
i=1,\cdots,\infty,j=0,\cdots,\infty
.
All other terms are classified as
\overline{\rm{BNSV}}
.
We use the overline notations to distiguish the
\vec{N}
-space approximations for those directly obtained in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space, because the inverse Mellin transform of

\overline{\rm{SV}}
terms in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space yields SV terms as well as NSV and BNSV terms.
Similarly, the inverse Mellin transform of
\overline{\rm{NSV}}
terms will produce
NSV and BNSV terms in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space.
The computation
G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}
is described in the App.
C
.

The Mellin moments of eq. (
43
) contain terms of the type

\omega=a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\beta_{0}\ln(N_{1}N_{2})
as well as single logarithms

\omega_{i}=a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\beta_{0}\ln(N_{i})
,
where large logarithms
\ln(N_{i})
and the strong coupling
a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})
at moderate values
combine into an effective expansion parameter of
{\cal O}(1)
, spoiling the reliability of perturbative predictions, if truncated at specific order in
a_{s}
.
A systematic reorganization of the
\vec{N}
-space in the exponent sums all the terms containing
\omega
,
\omega_{i}
etc using the resummed expression for strong coupling
a_{s}
as described in App.
E
.

By rewriting
G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}=\overline{G}_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}+G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}=\vec{1}}
and defining
\tilde{g}_{d,0}^{q}=\exp(G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}=\vec{1}})
, we obtain

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}^{\vec{N}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0}(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2})\exp\left(\overline{G}_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2})\right)\,.

(71)

We expand these
\vec{N}
-independent constants as
\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0}
=
\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{R}^{2})~\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,i}(Q^{2},\mu_{F}^{2},\mu_{R}^{2})
, so that the resummed result takes the following form,

\displaystyle\overline{G}_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}=

\displaystyle g^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)\ln{N_{1}}+\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}g^{q}_{d,i+2}(\omega)+\frac{1}{N_{1}}\overline{g}^{q}_{d,i}(\omega)\Bigg)+\frac{1}{N_{1}}\Bigg(h^{q}_{d,0}(\omega,N_{1})+\sum\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}h^{q}_{d,i}(\omega,\omega_{1},N_{1})\Bigg)

\displaystyle+(N_{1}\leftrightarrow N_{2},\omega_{1}\leftrightarrow\omega_{2})\,,

(72)

where

\displaystyle h^{q}_{d,0}(\omega,N_{l})

\displaystyle=h^{q}_{d,0,0}(\omega)+h^{q}_{d,0,1}(\omega)\ln N_{l}\,,

\displaystyle h^{q}_{d,i}(\omega,\omega_{l},N_{l})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{i-1}h^{q}_{d,i,k}(\omega,\omega_{l})~\ln^{k}N_{l}+\tilde{h}^{q}_{d,i,i}(\omega,\omega_{l})~\ln^{i}N_{l}\,.

(73)

The functions
g_{d,i}^{q}
and
\overline{g}_{d,i}^{q}
are derived from
\Phi^{\text{SV}}_{q}
, as given in eq. (
43
), and
h^{q}_{d,0}
and
h^{q}_{d,i}
originate from
\Phi^{\text{NSV}}_{q}
,
as given in eq. (
50
). These functions are listed in App.
H
.

V
Physical Evolution Kernel

The ingredients of SIDIS structure functions, CFs as well as the PDFs and FFs are defined in a factorization scheme,
but structure functions themselves, being physical observables are scheme independent.
This inherent scheme independence allows to derive a scheme-invariant combination of CFs and splitting functions by constructing a system of differential equations
among the structure functions
Furmanski and Petronzio 1982
;
Catani 1997
;
Blümlein et al. 2000
;
van Neerven and Vogt 2001
.
Such physical evolution equations have been employed previously in refs.
Grunberg and Ravindran 2009
;
Grunberg 2009
;
Grunberg 2010
to obtain certain NSV terms for the CFs of inclusive DIS.
With an ansatz based on the known results for the CFs and the AP kernels from fixed order computations,
predictions for the second and third orders CFs of structure functions in inclusive DIS were made
Grunberg 2010
, finding agreement for some of the color factors.
A systematic construction of physical evolution kernels (PEK), relying on the observation about the logarithmic structure of, namely the single-logarithmic enhancement in
(1-z)
at large
z
to all orders,
has been performed for the CFs of inclusive DIS, semi-inclusive
e^{+}e^{-}
annihilation and the DY process
in
Moch and Vogt 2009
(also see refs.
de Florian et al. 2014
;
Das et al. 2020b
).
By conjecturing that this behavior holds true at every order in
a_{s}
, constraints on the structure of the corresponding leading
\ln(1-z)
terms in the kernel could be derived and predictions for power suppressed
contributions, including NSV logarithms at higher orders in
a_{s}
, were obtained,
in agreement with known third-order results for DY and Higgs production
Anastasiou et al. 2015
;
Duhr et al. 2020
.

Following ref.
Moch and Vogt 2009
, we have established physical evolution equations for the structure functions
F_{1}
,
F_{2}
, and
g_{1}
to analyze the PEK in the SV and NSV limits.
In these limits, we have found that the evolution equations simplify significantly, with only diagonal splitting functions and diagonal CFs contributing to the PEK.
Setting
\mu_{F}^{2}=Q^{2}
for convenience, we find

\displaystyle Q^{2}\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}(g_{1})F_{J}

\displaystyle=\Bigg\{\frac{1}{2}(\Delta)P_{q\leftarrow q}(a_{s})+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}_{q\leftarrow q}(a_{s})+\beta(a_{s})\frac{d(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}(a_{s})}{da_{s}}\otimes\Big((\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq}(a_{s})\Big)^{-1}\Bigg\}\otimes(g_{1})F_{J}

\displaystyle=(\Delta)K_{J}~\otimes(g_{1})F_{J}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{\infty}a_{s}^{l+1}(\Delta)K_{J,l}~\otimes(g_{1})F_{J}\,,

(74)

where
a_{s}=a_{s}(Q^{2})
.
Note that the kernels
K_{J,l}
in the differential equations contain scheme-dependent CFs and splitting functions.
However, because of the scheme invariance of the structure functions, the kernels also have to be invariant under scheme transformations.
Using eq. (
10
), and expanding the QCD
\beta
function and the splitting functions in powers of
a_{s}
, we get,

\displaystyle K_{0}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}P^{q}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}^{q}_{0}\,,

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}P^{q}_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}^{q}_{1}-\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\,,

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}P^{q}_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}^{q}_{2}-\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}-2\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}+\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{2}\,,

\displaystyle K_{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}P^{q}_{3}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}^{q}_{3}-\beta_{2}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}-2\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}-3\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}+3\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}\otimes{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}+\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{2}-\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{3}\,,

\displaystyle K_{4}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}P^{q}_{4}+\frac{1}{2}\tilde{P}^{q}_{4}-\beta_{3}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}-2\beta_{2}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}-3\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}-4\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(4)}+4\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}\otimes{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}+2\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}\otimes^{2}

\displaystyle+3\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}\otimes{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}-4\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(2)}\otimes{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{2}+\beta_{2}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{2}-\beta_{1}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{3}+\beta_{0}{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{4}\,.

(75)

In the above, we have suppressed
\Delta
and the index
J
, and used a short-hand notation
{\cal C}^{(l)}_{q}
for

{\cal C}^{(l)}_{J,qq}
and
{\cal C}^{(l)}_{q}\otimes^{i}
, for the
(i-1)
-fold convolution of
{\cal C}_{q}^{(l)}
with itself,
i.e.,
{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes^{2}={\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}\otimes{\cal C}_{q}^{(1)}
etc.
Following the approach of ref.
Moch and Vogt 2009
and using CFs known to
second order
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
in
a_{s}
, we obtain
K_{l}
for
l=0,1,2
in
{\vec{N}}
-space, as

\displaystyle K_{0}

\displaystyle=\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{6-8\gamma_{E}\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{1}}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{2\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{2}}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{2\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{1}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\Bigg]-\bm{L_{1}^{1}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle K_{1}

\displaystyle=\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[-C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{34}{3}+\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{2}-\frac{80}{9}\gamma_{E}-\frac{16}{3}\gamma_{E}^{2}\bigg\}+C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{193}{3}-24\zeta_{3}+\frac{44}{3}\zeta_{2}-\frac{536}{9}\gamma_{E}+16\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}-\frac{88}{3}\gamma_{E}^{2}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{3+48\zeta_{3}-24\zeta_{2}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{1}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{56}{9}+\frac{8}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{332}{9}-4\zeta_{2}+\frac{44}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{12-16\gamma_{E}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{2}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{8}{9}+\frac{8}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{68}{9}-4\zeta_{2}+\frac{44}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{12-16\gamma_{E}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{1}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{40}{9}+\frac{16}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{-\frac{268}{9}+8\zeta_{2}-\frac{88}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{1}}{N_{2}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{16\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{1}}{N_{1}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{2}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bm{L_{1}^{1}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{40}{9}+\frac{16}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{268}{9}-8\zeta_{2}+\frac{88}{3}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{L_{1}^{1}L_{2}^{1}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{8}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{44}{3}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{1}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{1}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\bigg\{16\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{1}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{2}}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{4}{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\bigg\{\frac{22}{3}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle K_{2}

\displaystyle=\bm{L_{1}^{0}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{220}{9}-\frac{64}{27}\zeta_{3}+\frac{304}{27}\zeta_{2}-\frac{800}{81}\gamma_{E}-\frac{320}{27}\gamma_{E}^{2}-\frac{128}{27}\gamma_{E}^{3}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{3052}{9}-\frac{3440}{27}\zeta_{3}+\frac{4184}{27}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{80}{27}+\frac{32}{9}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{3560}{27}-\frac{88}{3}\zeta_{2}+\frac{968}{9}\gamma_{E}\bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}n_{f}\bigg\{4\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{1}}}\Bigg[C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{88}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{8}{9}\bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{242}{9}\bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}n_{f}\bigg\{16\bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}C_{A}\bigg\{88\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{0}}{N_{2}}}\Bigg[C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{88}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{8}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{242}{9}\bigg\}\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\bm{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}^{1}}\Bigg[C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{176}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{16}{9}\bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{484}{9}\bigg\}\Bigg]+\bm{L_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{0}}\Bigg[C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\bigg\{\frac{176}{27}\bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{16}{27}\bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\bigg\{\frac{484}{27}\bigg\}\Bigg]\,,

(76)

where
\bm{L_{j}^{i}}=\ln^{i}(N_{j})
, for
j=1,2
. From the above results for
K_{i},i=0,1,2
, we observe that

•

In the SV part of
K_{J,l}
, the highest power of logarithms
N_{i}
is constrained, for example for the logarithms
\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
, we find
0\leq i+j\leq l+1
.

•

In the NSV part of
K_{J,l}
, the power of highest logarithms is constrained, for example, for the logarithms
\frac{1}{N_{1}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
or
\frac{1}{N_{2}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
, we have the limit on
i,j
as
0\leq i+j\leq l
.

Thus, also the SIDIS PEK shows a single-logarithmic enhancement, both in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space as well as in
\vec{N}
space, similar to what was observed in ref.
Moch and Vogt 2009
in the case of PEKs from inclusive cross-sections.
If we conjecture that this logarithm structure persists at every order in
a_{s}
for
K_{J,l}
, then we can determine certain SV and NSV logarithms at a given order
l
for
{\cal C}_{q}^{(l)}
from the knowledge of AP kernels and CFs from previous order in
a_{s}
, i.e., results for all orders up to
a_{s}^{k}
where
k<l
.

The validation of the SIDIS PEK predictions against the known second-order CFs can be readily performed.
We use
K_{0}
and
K_{1}
, i.e., the PEKs known from the NLO CFs and NLO splitting functions.
Then, for SV terms, the maximum sum of logarithmic powers is
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=3

for terms of the form
\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
.
For NSV terms, the constraint is
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=2
for logarithms of the form

\frac{1}{N_{1}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
or
\frac{1}{N_{2}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
.
By substituting explicit results for
P_{2}^{q},\tilde{P}_{2}^{q}
and
\mathcal{C}_{q}^{(1)}

in
K_{2}
of eq. (
V
), and applying the general structure of SV and NSV terms
specified in eq. (
II
),
we impose the condition that all terms with
i+j>3
for SV and
i+j>2
for NSV must vanish.
This process yields a set of 13 equations relating the second-order
coefficients
\mathcal{C}_{J}^{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},(2)}
in eq. (
II
).
The solutions to these equations provide the NNLO coefficients for terms such as

\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}
with
i=3
and
\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}
with
i+j=2
, along with similar terms where
{x}^{\prime}\leftrightarrow{z}^{\prime}
.
We find that these predictions are in complete agreement with the results obtained from explicit computations
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
.

Next, by using known NNLO results, we can predict the third and fourth-order results for CFs, i.e., classes of logarithms for
{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}
and
{\cal C}_{q}^{(4)}
.
For SV terms in
K_{3}
, we consider logarithms of the form

\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
with the constraint
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=4
, and for NSV logarithms

\frac{1}{N_{1}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
or
\frac{1}{N_{2}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
,
the constraint is
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=3
.
By imposing that there are no SV logarithms for
i+j>4
and no NSV logarithms exist for
i+j>3
,
we obtain a system of 35 linear equations that relate the coefficients
C_{J}^{\xi_{1}\xi_{2},(3)}
. Solving for these coefficients, we can predict 35 different logarithms at N
3
LO level.
For example, at third order, for the CF
{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}
in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
-space,
we can predict terms such as

\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}
for
i=4,5
and
\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}

for
i+j=3,4
and similarly for
{x}^{\prime}\leftrightarrow{z}^{\prime}
.
We have found that all these predictions are in complete agreement with those obtained using the resummation approach.

Similarly, we can determine the coefficients of highest logarithms in
{\cal C}_{q}^{(4)}
by observing that the SV logarithms of the form

\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
are constrained by
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=5
and for NSV logarithms,

\frac{1}{N_{1}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
or
\frac{1}{N_{2}}\ln^{i}(N_{1})\ln^{j}(N_{2})
,
the constraint is
(i+j)|_{\rm max}=4
.
These constraints yield a system of 66 equations, with which we can predict 47 SV and NSV logarithms in
{\cal C}_{q}^{(4)}
, namely terms of the form
\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{L}^{i}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}
for
i=6,7
and
\{\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{D}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}},\mathcal{D}^{i}_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\mathcal{L}^{j}_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}\}
for
i+j=5,6
, including analogous terms where
{x}^{\prime}\leftrightarrow{z}^{\prime}
.
These predictions are also in agreement with the ones obtained via the resummation approach.
However, the remaining 19 equations require information from
{\cal C}_{q}^{(3)}
and hence we cannot predict them using this current approach.

VI
Phenomenology

We are now in the position to illustrate the impact of resummed SV and NSV contributions on the structure functions
F_{1}
and
g_{1}
for the EIC at a center-of-mass energy of
\sqrt{s}
= 140 GeV.
To that end, we consider the logarithmic accuracies
\overline{\text{LL}}
,
\overline{\text{NLL}}
and
\overline{\text{NNLL}}
.
As shown in the first row of Tab.
3
,
the resummed result at
\overline{\text{LL}}
accuracy requires the resummed exponents
\{\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,0},g^{q}_{d,1},\overline{g}^{q}_{d,0}
,
h^{q}_{d,0}\}
.
They systematically resum SV logarithms of the form
\{\ln^{i}N_{1}\ln^{j}N_{2}\}

(where
i+j=2n
) and NSV logarithms like

\{{\ln^{i}N_{1}\over N_{1}}\ln^{j}N_{2},{\ln^{i}N_{2}\over N_{2}}\ln^{j}N_{1}\}

(where
i+j=2n-1
) at
n
th order in
a_{s}
, i.e.,
a_{s}^{n}
for all
n>1
:

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq,\vec{N}}^{\overline{\rm{LL}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,0}\Big)\exp\bigg[g^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)\ln N_{1}+\frac{1}{N_{1}}\bigg(\overline{g}^{q}_{d,0}(\omega)+h^{q}_{d,0}(\omega,N_{1})\bigg)+(N_{1}\leftrightarrow N_{2})\bigg]\,.

(77)

Similarly, for the resummed result at
\overline{\text{NLL}}
accuracy, as detailed
in the second row of Tab.
3
, we require
the additional resummation exponents
\{\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,1},g^{q}_{d,2},\overline{g}^{q}_{d,1},h^{q}_{d,1}\}
alongside the
\overline{\text{LL}}
exponents.
This suffices to sum up the next-to-leading SV logarithms
and next-to-highest NSV logarithms to all orders.
Specifically, this includes SV logarithms of the form
\{\ln^{i}N_{1}\ln^{j}N_{2}\}

(where
i+j=\{2n-1,2n-2\}
)
and NSV logarithms of the form

\{{\ln^{i}N_{1}\over N_{1}}\ln^{j}N_{2},{\ln^{i}N_{2}\over N_{2}}\ln^{j}N_{1}\}

(where
i+j=2n-2
with
n>2
), i.e.,

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq,\vec{N}}^{\overline{\rm{NLL}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,0}+a_{s}~\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,1}\Big)\exp\bigg[g^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)\ln N_{1}+\frac{1}{2}g^{q}_{d,2}(\omega)+\frac{1}{N_{1}}\bigg(\overline{g}^{q}_{d,0}(\omega)+a_{s}~\overline{g}^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)+h^{q}_{d,0}(\omega,N_{1})

\displaystyle+a_{s}~h^{q}_{d,1}(\omega,\omega_{1},N_{1})\bigg)+(N_{1}\leftrightarrow N_{2},\omega_{1}\leftrightarrow\omega_{2})\bigg]\,.

(78)

The set of resummed exponents required to predict the tower of NSV logarithm in
{\cal C}_{J,qq,\vec{N}}^{(n)}
,
L_{1}^{i}=\ln^{i}(N_{1})
,

,

,

.

According to the third row of Tab.
3
,
we include the resummation exponents

\{\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,2},g^{q}_{d,3},\overline{g}^{q}_{d,2},h^{q}_{d,2}\}

alongside the
\overline{\rm{LL}}
and
\overline{\rm{NLL}}
terms to achieve
\overline{\rm{NNLL}}
accuracy.
This set resums SV logarithms of
the form
\{\ln^{i}N_{1}\ln^{j}N_{2}\}
(where
i+j=\{2n-3,2n-4\}
) and
NSV logarithms of the form
\{{\ln^{i}N_{1}\over N_{1}}\ln^{j}N_{2},{\ln^{i}N_{2}\over N_{2}}\ln^{j}N_{1}\}

with
i+j=2n-3,n>3
.

\displaystyle(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,qq,\vec{N}}^{\overline{\rm{NNLL}}}

\displaystyle=\Big(\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,0}+a_{s}~\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,1}+a_{s}^{2}~\tilde{g}^{q}_{d,0,2}\Big)\exp\bigg[g^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)\ln N_{1}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(g^{q}_{d,2}(\omega)+a_{s}~g^{q}_{d,3}(\omega)\bigg)+\frac{1}{N_{1}}\bigg(\overline{g}^{q}_{d,0}(\omega)+a_{s}~\overline{g}^{q}_{d,1}(\omega)

\displaystyle+a_{s}^{2}~\overline{g}^{q}_{d,2}(\omega)+h^{q}_{d,0}(\omega,N_{1})+a_{s}~h^{q}_{d,1}(\omega,\omega_{1},N_{1})+a_{s}^{2}~h^{q}_{d,2}(\omega,\omega_{1},N_{1})\bigg)+(N_{1}\leftrightarrow N_{2},\omega_{1}\leftrightarrow\omega_{2})\bigg]\,.

(79)

The resummed results are matched to the respective fixed-order results as follows:

\displaystyle(g_{1}){F}_{J}^{\rm N^{n}LO+\overline{\rm{N^{n}LL}}}

\displaystyle=(g_{1}){F}_{J}^{\rm N^{n}LO}

\displaystyle+\sum_{cc}\,\int_{c_{1}-i\infty}^{c_{1}+i\infty}\frac{dN_{1}}{2\pi i}\int_{c_{2}-i\infty}^{c_{2}+i\infty}\frac{dN_{2}}{2\pi i}~(\Delta)f_{c,N_{1}}(\mu_{F}^{2})D_{c,N_{2}}(\mu_{F}^{2})\times\bigg((\Delta){\cal C}_{J,cc,\vec{N}}^{\overline{\rm{N^{n}LL}}}-(\Delta){\cal C}_{J,cc,\vec{N}}\bigg|_{\text{tr}^{n}}\bigg)\,.

(80)

The first term in eq. (
80
) represents the fixed-order contributions up to
N
n
LO accuracy. The second term corresponds to the resummed result
at
\overline{\text{N}^{\text{n}}\text{LL}}
accuracy.
This term is derived by subtracting its fixed-order expansion, truncated up to order
a_{s}^{n}
,
from the full resummed result, which precisely avoids double-counting of threshold logarithms.
Therefore, the latter encapsulates the all-order SV and NSV resummed contributions
in perturbation theory starting from
a^{n+1}_{s}
onward.
The subscript ‘tr
n
’ denotes the
n^{\rm th}
-order ‘truncated’ part of the resummed result
which is already contained in the fixed-order expressions.
The CFs required for
(g_{1})F_{J}^{\rm{N^{n}LO}}
in the first term are known up to NNLO
Altarelli et al. 1979
;
Goyal et al. 2024a
;
Bonino et al. 2024a
;
Goyal et al. 2024b
;
Bonino et al. 2024b
;
Goyal et al. 2025
;
Bonino et al. 2025
.

For the unpolarized structure function (
F_{1}
), we have employed the
ABMP16
PDF sets
Alekhin et al. 2017

and
NNFF10PIp
FF sets
Bertone et al. 2017
at their respective orders,
except for the LO where we use NLO PDF set.
For the polarized structure function (
g_{1}
), we have used the polarized
BDSSV24NLO
PDFs at LL and NLL accuracies, and
BDSSV24NNLO
PDFs at NNLL accuracy.
The strong coupling
a_{s}
is taken as provided through the
LHAPDF
interface
Buckley et al. 2015
with

n_{f}
= 3 active massless quark flavors throughout.
We have used an in-house
FORTRAN
code to perform the double Mellin inversion for the resummed contributions, and we have adopted the so-called minimal prescription
Catani et al. 1996
to handle the Landau pole in the Mellin inversion routines.

\overline{\text{LL}}

or

VII
Conclusions

Threshold-enhanced logarithms, present in the fixed order predictions, need to be resummed in order to obtain reliable phenomenological predictions in the relavant kinematic range.
SIDIS is characterized by the scaling variables
x^{\prime}
, or
z^{\prime}
, which tned to unity in threshold region.
Fixed-order perturbative predictions of the CFs are then dominated by the plus and delta function distributions (SV terms) as well as
\text{log}^{j}(1-x^{\prime})
or
\text{log}^{j}(1-z^{\prime})
(NSV terms) with
j=0,1,2,\cdots
.
This is a situation similar to the case of rapidity distributions in the DY process.
Based on the insights there, we have developed a formalism, using collinear factorisation and renormalisation group equations, to achieve the resummation of these threshold logarithms (SV as well as NSV) both in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space as well as in the space of the conjugate Mellin variables
\vec{N}=(N_{1},N_{2})
.
In Mellin space, these threshold logarithms show up as
\log^{i}(N_{1})\log^{j}(N_{2})
for SV as well as
\log^{i}(N_{1})\log^{j}(N_{2})/N_{2}
,
\log^{i}(N_{1})\log^{j}(N_{2})/N_{1}
where
\{i,j\}=0,1,2,\cdots
and they can be systematically resummed to all orders in
a_{s}
.

The compact expressions for the resummed results encapsulate both SV and NSV logarithms in
\vec{N}
-space and allow for phenomenological studies with EIC kinematics.
Upon expansion in
a_{s}
the resummed result generates predictions for the full SV and partial NSV terms for the CFs
(\Delta)\mathcal{C}^{(3),\text{SV}}_{J,qq}
,

(\Delta)\mathcal{C}^{(3),\text{NSV}}_{J,qq}

third order in
(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})
space.
At fourth order, using the recently obtained results for four-loop quark virtual and eikonal anomalous dimensions
B_{4}^{q}
and
f_{4}^{q}
,
we have predicted all SV terms in
(\Delta)\mathcal{C}^{(4),\text{SV}}_{J,qq}
except the coefficient of
\delta_{\overline{x}^{\prime}}\delta_{\overline{z}^{\prime}}

The derivation of the NSV terms has been corrobrated by an independent approach that uses PEKs for the structure functions, and
we have confirmed our predictions both at third and fourth order. Finally, we have demonstrated the numerical importance of the resummed threshold logarithms for structure functions
F_{1}
and
g_{1}
at EIC energies.
We have shown, that at each logarithmic order the resummed contributions are larger than the corresponding fixed order ones.
Moreover, including these logarithms to all orders through resummation reduces the dependence on the renormalisation and factorisation scales and hence improves the reliability of our predictions.

Appendix A
Coefficients in
\Gamma_{\hat{F}_{q}}
:
K_{d}^{q}
and
{G}_{d}^{q}

The coefficients
K_{d}^{q}
to fourth order in
\hat{a}_{s}
are given below:

\displaystyle K_{d}^{q}(\mu_{R}^{2},\varepsilon)=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{R}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\bigg)^{i\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}K^{q(i)}_{d}(\varepsilon)\,,

(81)

where

\displaystyle{K}^{q(1)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{-2A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{K}^{q(2)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{2\beta_{0}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{-A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{K}^{q(3)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg\{-\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}^{2}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{\frac{2}{3}\beta_{1}A^{q}_{1}+\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{-\frac{2}{3}A^{q}_{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{K}^{q(4)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Bigg\{4\beta_{0}^{3}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg\{-\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}A^{q}_{1}-6\beta_{0}^{2}A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{3}\beta_{2}A^{q}_{1}+\beta_{1}A^{q}_{2}+3\beta_{0}A^{q}_{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{-\frac{1}{2}A^{q}_{4}\Bigg\}\,.

(82)

The constants
A^{q}_{i}
are the
a_{s}^{i}
coefficients of the cusp anomalous dimension
A^{q}
:

A^{q}(a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2}))=\sum_{i}~a_{s}^{i}(\mu_{R}^{2})~A^{q}_{i}\,.

(83)

The finite terms
G^{q}_{d}
can be written as

\displaystyle G_{d}^{q}(\hat{a}_{s},Q^{2},\mu_{R}^{2},\varepsilon)=G_{d}^{q}(a_{s}(Q^{2}),\varepsilon)+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\bigg(\frac{Q^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\bigg)^{i\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}~K_{d}^{q,(i)}(\varepsilon)-K_{d}^{q}(\mu_{R}^{2},\varepsilon)\,.

(84)

The first terms is expanded as:
G_{d}^{q}(a_{s}(Q^{2}),\varepsilon)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}(Q^{2}){G}_{d,i}^{q}(\varepsilon)
, where

\displaystyle{G}^{q}_{d,i}(\varepsilon)=2B^{q}_{i}+f_{i}^{q}+\chi_{d,i}^{q}+\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\varepsilon^{j}{\cal G}^{q,(j)}_{d,i}\,,

(85)

with

\displaystyle\chi^{q}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=0\,,\chi^{q}_{d,2}=-2\beta_{0}{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}\,,\chi^{q}_{d,3}=-2\beta_{1}{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}-2\beta_{0}\left({\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}+2\beta_{0}{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\chi^{q}_{d,4}

\displaystyle=-2\beta_{2}{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}-2\beta_{1}\left({\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}+4\beta_{0}{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}\right)-2\beta_{0}\left({\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,3}+2\beta_{0}{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,2}+4\beta_{0}^{2}{\cal G}^{q,(3)}_{d,1}\right)\,.

(86)

Next, the results for
{\cal G}^{q,(j)}_{d,i}
are given below

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{-8+\zeta_{2}\Bigg\}\,,\quad\quad{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,1}=C_{F}\Bigg\{8-\frac{7}{3}\zeta_{3}-\frac{3}{4}\zeta_{2}\Bigg\}\,,\quad\quad{\cal G}^{q,(3)}_{d,1}=C_{F}\Bigg\{-8+\frac{7}{4}\zeta_{3}+\zeta_{2}+\frac{47}{80}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(4)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{8-\frac{31}{20}\zeta_{5}-\frac{7}{3}\zeta_{3}-\zeta_{2}+\frac{7}{24}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{141}{320}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(5)}_{d,1}

\displaystyle=C_{F}\Bigg\{-8+\frac{93}{80}\zeta_{5}+\frac{7}{3}\zeta_{3}-\frac{49}{144}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\zeta_{2}-\frac{7}{32}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{47}{80}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{949}{4480}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{5813}{162}-\frac{8}{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{37}{9}\zeta_{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{70165}{324}-\frac{260}{3}\zeta_{3}+\frac{575}{18}\zeta_{2}-\frac{88}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{4}+60\zeta_{3}-58\zeta_{2}+\frac{88}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(2)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{-\frac{129389}{1944}+\frac{301}{27}\zeta_{3}-\frac{425}{54}\zeta_{2}+\frac{7}{12}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{109}{16}-12\zeta_{5}-184\zeta_{3}+\frac{437}{4}\zeta_{2}+28\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{108}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{1547797}{3888}-51\zeta_{5}-\frac{12479}{54}\zeta_{3}+\frac{7297}{108}\zeta_{2}+\frac{89}{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{653}{24}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(3)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{2628821}{23328}-6\zeta_{5}-\frac{4085}{162}\zeta_{3}+\frac{8405}{648}\zeta_{2}-\frac{11}{6}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{1873}{720}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{31174909}{46656}-105\zeta_{5}-\frac{154405}{324}\zeta_{3}

\displaystyle+\frac{569}{6}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{155701}{1296}\zeta_{2}+\frac{239}{12}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{100907}{1440}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{809}{140}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}+C_{F}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{1287}{64}-9\zeta_{5}-\frac{1807}{4}\zeta_{3}+134\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{2991}{16}\zeta_{2}

\displaystyle+13\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{127}{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{858}{35}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(4)}_{d,2}

\displaystyle=C_{F}n_{f}\Bigg\{-\frac{50947325}{279936}+\frac{3109}{180}\zeta_{5}+\frac{91985}{1944}\zeta_{3}-\frac{289}{108}\zeta_{3}^{2}-\frac{160493}{7776}\zeta_{2}+\frac{791}{216}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{12781}{2160}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{953}{480}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{599567077}{559872}+93\zeta_{7}-\frac{96503}{360}\zeta_{5}-\frac{3643249}{3888}\zeta_{3}+\frac{32339}{216}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{3110773}{15552}\zeta_{2}+\frac{497}{20}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}+\frac{16739}{432}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}

\displaystyle-\frac{619829}{4320}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{7103}{120}\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{7073}{1344}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{10901}{256}+\frac{675}{2}\zeta_{7}-99\zeta_{5}-\frac{50219}{48}\zeta_{3}+\frac{373}{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{19405}{64}\zeta_{2}+3\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}

\displaystyle+\frac{235}{6}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{24453}{160}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{2597}{30}\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{123}{4}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle{\cal G}^{q,(1)}_{d,3}

\displaystyle=C_{F}N_{4}n_{fv}\Bigg\{12-80\zeta_{5}+14\zeta_{3}+30\zeta_{2}-\frac{6}{5}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}-C_{F}n_{f}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{258445}{2187}-\frac{536}{81}\zeta_{3}+\frac{3466}{81}\zeta_{2}+\frac{40}{9}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}C_{A}^{2}\Bigg\{\frac{48902713}{8748}-\frac{688}{3}\zeta_{5}-\frac{85883}{18}\zeta_{3}+\frac{1136}{3}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{1083305}{486}\zeta_{2}-\frac{1786}{9}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{37271}{90}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{6152}{63}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}^{2}C_{A}\Bigg\{\frac{230}{3}-\frac{3020}{3}\zeta_{5}-\frac{23402}{9}\zeta_{3}+296\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{55499}{18}\zeta_{2}-\frac{3448}{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+\frac{2432}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}-\frac{15448}{105}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle-C_{F}^{3}\Bigg\{\frac{1527}{4}-1992\zeta_{5}+2130\zeta_{3}-48\zeta_{3}^{2}+206\zeta_{2}-840\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+534\zeta_{2}^{2}-\frac{21584}{105}\zeta_{2}^{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}C_{A}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{3702974}{2187}-72\zeta_{5}-\frac{68660}{81}\zeta_{3}+\frac{155008}{243}\zeta_{2}+\frac{392}{9}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{1298}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+C_{F}^{2}n_{f}\Bigg\{\frac{73271}{162}-\frac{368}{3}\zeta_{5}+\frac{19700}{27}\zeta_{3}-\frac{7541}{18}\zeta_{2}-\frac{152}{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}-\frac{704}{45}\zeta_{2}^{2}\Bigg\}\,.

(87)

Appendix B
Coefficients in
\Phi^{\text{SV}}_{q}
:
\overline{K}^{q}_{d}
and
\overline{G}^{q}_{d}

Here we present the SV coefficients in
\Phi_{q}
.
We expand
\overline{K}_{d}^{q}(\mu_{R}^{2},x^{\prime},z^{\prime},\varepsilon)=\delta(1-x^{\prime})\delta(1-z^{\prime})\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\bigg(\frac{\mu_{R}^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\bigg)^{i\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}S_{\varepsilon}^{i}\overline{K}^{q(i)}_{d}(\varepsilon)
to fourth order in
a_{s}
, where

\displaystyle\overline{K}^{q(1)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{2A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{K}^{q(2)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{-2\beta_{0}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{K}^{q(3)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg\{\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}^{2}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{-\frac{2}{3}\beta_{1}A^{q}_{1}-\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{2}{3}A^{q}_{3}\Bigg\}\,,

\displaystyle\overline{K}^{q(4)}_{d}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{4}}\Bigg\{-4\beta_{0}^{3}A^{q}_{1}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg\{\frac{8}{3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}A^{q}_{1}+6\beta_{0}^{2}A^{q}_{2}\Bigg\}+\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg\{-\frac{1}{3}\beta_{2}A^{q}_{1}-\beta_{1}A^{q}_{2}-3\beta_{0}A^{q}_{3}\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Bigg\{\frac{1}{2}A^{q}_{4}\Bigg\}\,.

(88)

The finite terms
\overline{G}_{d,\text{SV}}^{q(i)}(\varepsilon)
are related to its renormalised counter parts
\overline{\cal G}_{d,i}^{q}(\varepsilon)
in the following way:

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\hat{a}_{s}^{i}\left(\frac{Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})}{\mu^{2}}\right)^{i\frac{\varepsilon}{2}}S^{i}_{\varepsilon}~\overline{G}_{d,\text{SV}}^{q,(i)}(\varepsilon)=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i}\left(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\right)\overline{{\cal G}}^{q}_{d,i}(\varepsilon)\,.

(89)

we find,

\displaystyle\overline{G}^{q(1)}_{d,\text{SV}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\overline{G}^{q(2)}_{d,\text{SV}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg(-2\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+\overline{\cal G}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\overline{G}^{q(3)}_{d,\text{SV}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg(4\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg(-\beta_{1}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)-4\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+\overline{\cal G}_{d,3}^{q}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle\overline{G}^{q(4)}_{d,\text{SV}}(\varepsilon)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{1\over\varepsilon^{3}}\Bigg(-8\beta_{0}^{3}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+{1\over\varepsilon^{2}}\Bigg({16\over 3}\beta_{0}\beta_{1}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)+12\beta_{0}^{2}\overline{\cal G}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)

(90)

\displaystyle+{1\over\varepsilon}\Bigg(-{2\over 3}\beta_{2}\overline{\cal G}_{d,1}^{q}(\varepsilon)-2\beta_{1}\overline{\cal G}_{d,2}^{q}(\varepsilon)-6\beta_{0}\overline{\cal G}_{d,3}^{q}(\varepsilon)\Bigg)+\overline{\cal G}_{d,4}^{q}(\varepsilon)\,.

Appendix C
Details of Mellin moment

Here we describe how to obtain the Mellin moment of
\mathrm{\Psi}_{d}^{q}
given in eq. (
II
). We decompose the Mellin moment of
\mathrm{\Psi}_{d}^{q}
as

\displaystyle G_{d,q}^{\vec{N}}

\displaystyle={\cal N}\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}\Psi_{d}^{q}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\Psi^{q,\vec{N}}_{d}+\ln\Big(g_{d,0}^{q}\big(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2})\big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\Bigg[\bigg(\sum_{i=1}^{6}\psi^{d}_{i}\bigg)+(N_{1}\leftrightarrow N_{2},L^{q}\leftrightarrow\tilde{L}^{q})\Bigg]+\ln\Big(g_{d,0}^{q}\big(a_{s}(\mu_{F}^{2})\big)\Big)\,,

(91)

where the symbol
{\cal N}
indicates we need to keep only
SV
and
NSV
terms. The terms
\psi_{i}^{d}
in the above equation are given below:

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{1}(N_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{A^{q}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}}+L^{q}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),(1-x^{\prime})\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{2}(N_{1})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\Bigg(\frac{G^{q}_{d}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})\big)\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}}+\varphi_{d,q,f}^{q}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})\big),(1-x^{\prime})\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{3}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}\Bigg(\frac{A^{q}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big)\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}(1-z^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{4}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}~Q^{2}\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{G^{q}_{d}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big)\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}(1-z^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{5}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}\Bigg(\frac{L^{q}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big),(1-z^{\prime})\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{6}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}~Q^{2}\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{\varphi^{q}_{d,q,f}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big),(1-z^{\prime})\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)\,.

Following
Laenen et al. 2008
, we replace,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\bigg(\frac{{\xi^{N-1}-1}}{1-\xi}\bigg)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\left(N{\frac{d}{dN}}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}~\theta\left(1-\frac{1}{N}-\xi\right)

(92)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\xi^{N-1}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\frac{1}{N}\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\left(N{\frac{d}{dN}}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}~\theta\left(1-\frac{1}{N}-\xi\right)

(93)

Here,
\xi
can be
x^{\prime}
or
z^{\prime}
and corresponding
N
can be
N_{1}
or
N_{2}
.
See App.
D
for the expansion of
\hat{\Gamma}_{A}(N\frac{d}{dN})
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}(N\frac{d}{dN})
operators.
We need to apply
\hat{\Gamma}_{A}
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}
on the integrals through their expansions in terms of
N\frac{d}{dN}
.
This can be done only after making an appropriate change of variables and interchange of the order of the integrals.
For type-
\psi^{d}_{1}
,

\displaystyle\psi_{1}^{d}(N)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\xi^{N-1}\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\Bigg(\frac{F^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)}{(1-\xi)_{+}}+\tilde{F}^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),(1-\xi)\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\bigg(\frac{\xi^{N-1}-1}{1-\xi}\bigg)\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}F^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)+\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\xi^{N-1}\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\tilde{F}^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),(1-\xi)\Big)

\displaystyle\underset{{\tiny N\rightarrow\infty}}{=}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}F^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}\int_{\mu_{F}^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\tilde{F}^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),(1-\xi)\Big)\,.

(94)

To simplify further we set
\mu_{F}^{2}=Q^{2}
and perform a change of variables by introducing
\mu^{2}=Q^{2}(1-\xi)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}\int_{Q^{2}}^{Q^{2}(1-\xi)}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow-\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\int^{Q^{2}}_{\mu^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\,,

and interchange the order of integrations,

\displaystyle\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\int^{Q^{2}}_{\mu^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}=\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int^{\lambda^{2}}_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\,.

These manipulations lead to

\displaystyle\psi_{1}^{d}(N)

\displaystyle\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{=}-\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int^{\lambda^{2}}_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{1}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)-\frac{1}{N}\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int^{\lambda^{2}}_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\tilde{F}^{d}_{1}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\mu^{2}}{Q^{2}}\bigg)

(95)

For type-
\psi^{d}_{2}
,

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{2}(N)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\xi^{N-1}\Bigg(\frac{F^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big)\Big)}{(1-\xi)_{+}}+\tilde{F}^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big),(1-\xi)\Big)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\Bigg(\frac{\xi^{N-1}-1}{1-\xi}\Bigg)F^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big)\Big)+\int_{0}^{1}d\xi\xi^{N-1}\tilde{F}^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big),(1-\xi)\Big)

\displaystyle\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{=}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}F^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{N}\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N}}\frac{d\xi}{1-\xi}\tilde{F}^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-\xi)\big),(1-\xi)\Big)\,.

(96)

Changing the integration variable from
\xi\rightarrow\lambda^{2}=Q^{2}(1-\xi)
we obtain

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{2}(N)

\displaystyle\underset{N\rightarrow\infty}{=}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}F^{d}_{2}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)+\frac{1}{N}\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N\frac{d}{dN}\Big)\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\tilde{F}^{d}_{2}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\lambda^{2}}{Q^{2}}\bigg)

(97)

Note that in
\psi^{d}_{4}
and
\psi^{d}_{6}
after replacing
Q^{2}\frac{d}{dQ^{2}}
by
\bigg(\beta\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big)\Big)\times\frac{d}{da_{s}}\bigg)
, it becomes similar to
\psi^{d}_{3}
and
\psi^{d}_{5}
respectively.

For type-
\psi^{d}_{3}
,

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{3}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}\Bigg(\frac{F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big)\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}(1-z^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\underset{N_{2}\rightarrow\infty}{\underset{N_{1}\rightarrow\infty}{=}}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\Big)\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{2}\frac{d}{dN_{2}}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N_{1}}}\frac{dx^{\prime}}{1-x^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N_{2}}}\frac{dz^{\prime}}{1-z^{\prime}}F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big)\Big)\,.

(98)

After an appropriate change of variables we get,

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{3}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle\underset{N_{2}\rightarrow\infty}{\underset{N_{1}\rightarrow\infty}{=}}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\Big)\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{2}\frac{d}{dN_{2}}\Big)\Bigg[\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)-\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle-\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)+\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{3}\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big)\Big)\Bigg]\,.

(99)

For type-
\psi^{d}_{5}
,

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{5}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}dx^{\prime}x^{\prime N_{1}-1}\int_{0}^{1}dz^{\prime}z^{\prime N_{2}-1}\Bigg(\frac{F^{d}_{5}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big),(1-z^{\prime})\Big)}{(1-x^{\prime})_{+}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\underset{N_{2}\rightarrow\infty}{\underset{N_{1}\rightarrow\infty}{=}}\frac{1}{N_{2}}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\Big)\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N_{2}\frac{d}{dN_{2}}\Big)\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N_{1}}}\frac{dx^{\prime}}{1-x^{\prime}}\int_{0}^{1-\frac{1}{N_{2}}}\frac{dz^{\prime}}{1-z^{\prime}}F^{d}_{5}\Big(a_{s}\big(Q^{2}(1-x^{\prime})(1-z^{\prime})\big),(1-z^{\prime})\Big)\,.

(100)

After a change of variables we get

\displaystyle\psi^{d}_{5}(N_{1},N_{2})

\displaystyle\underset{N_{2}\rightarrow\infty}{\underset{N_{1}\rightarrow\infty}{=}}\frac{1}{N_{2}}\hat{\Gamma}_{A}\Big(N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\Big)\hat{\Gamma}_{B}\Big(N_{2}\frac{d}{dN_{2}}\Big)\Bigg[\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{5}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\mu^{2}N_{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle-\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}N_{2}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{5}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\mu^{2}N_{2}}\bigg)-\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{\lambda^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{5}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\mu^{2}}{Q^{2}}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{2}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\int_{\frac{Q^{2}}{N_{1}}}^{Q^{2}}\frac{d\mu^{2}}{\mu^{2}}F^{d}_{5}\bigg(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),\frac{\lambda^{2}}{\mu^{2}}\bigg)\Bigg]\,.

(101)

For all
\psi_{i}^{d}
, note that the
\mu^{2}
integral is easy to perform.
Before carrying out the
\lambda^{2}
integral we apply
\hat{\Gamma}_{A}
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}
using Leibniz integral rule, for e.g.,

\displaystyle N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\int_{g_{l}(N_{1},N_{2})}^{g_{u}(N_{1},N_{2})}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{d\ln(g_{u})}{d\ln N_{1}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(g_{u}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)-\frac{d\ln(g_{l})}{d\ln N_{1}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(g_{l}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle+\int_{g_{l}(N_{1},N_{2})}^{g_{u}(N_{1},N_{2})}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\partial}{\partial\ln N_{1}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)\,,

and generally

\displaystyle\left(N_{1}\frac{d}{dN_{1}}\right)^{n}\int_{g_{l}(N_{1},N_{2})}^{g_{u}(N_{1},N_{2})}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\Bigg[D^{n-j}_{u,1}~\bigg(\frac{d\ln(g_{u})}{d\ln N_{1}}\times\frac{\partial^{j-1}}{\partial(\ln N_{1})^{j-1}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)\Bigg|_{\lambda^{2}=g_{u}}\bigg)

\displaystyle-D^{n-j}_{l,1}~\bigg(\frac{d\ln(g_{l})}{d\ln N_{1}}\times\frac{\partial^{j-1}}{\partial(\ln N_{1})^{j-1}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)\Bigg|_{\lambda^{2}=g_{l}}\bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle+\int_{g_{l}(N_{1},N_{2})}^{g_{u}(N_{1},N_{2})}\frac{d\lambda^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\frac{\partial^{n}}{\partial(\ln N_{1})^{n}}F\Big(a_{s}\big(\lambda^{2}\big),N_{1},N_{2}\Big)\,,

(102)

where
D_{k,1}^{i}
for
k=l,u
is defined as

\displaystyle D_{k,1}^{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\partial}{\partial\ln N_{1}}-\frac{d\ln(g_{k})}{d\ln N_{1}}\beta\big(a_{s}(g_{k})\big)\frac{\partial}{\partial a_{s}(g_{k})}\right)^{i}\,,

which is used to deal with
N_{1}
dependence appearing in the argument of
a_{s}\big(g_{k}(N_{1},N_{2})\big)
.
The integral over
\lambda^{2}
is done after replacing
a_{s}(\lambda^{2})
by eq. (
E
) and changing variables from
\lambda^{2}\rightarrow l=1+\beta_{0}a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\ln(\lambda^{2}/\mu_{R}^{2})
.
While substituting the limits we have used
\omega,\omega_{1},\omega_{2}
variables where,
\omega=a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\beta_{0}\ln(N_{1}N_{2})
and
\omega_{l}=a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\beta_{0}\ln N_{l}
for
l=1,2
.

Appendix D
Expansion of
\hat{\Gamma}_{A}(x)
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}(x)

In this section we present the expansion of
\hat{\Gamma}_{A}(x)
and
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}(x)
used in App.
C
.

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{A}(x)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma_{k}^{A}(x)^{k}\,,

(103)

where the coefficients
\gamma_{k}^{A}
are given as

\displaystyle\gamma_{k}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma_{k}(N)}{k!}(-1)^{k}\,.

(104)

For the definition of
\Gamma_{k}(N)
, see eq. (25) of
Laenen et al. 2008
. We get

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{A}

\displaystyle=1\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{A}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{E}-\frac{1}{2N}\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}+\zeta_{2}\bigg)-\frac{1}{2N}\bigg(1+\gamma_{E}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\gamma_{E}^{3}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{3}\zeta_{3}-\frac{1}{4N}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}+2\gamma_{E}+\zeta_{2}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\gamma_{E}^{4}+\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{9}{40}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{3}\bigg)-\frac{1}{12N}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}+3\gamma_{E}^{2}+3\zeta_{2}+3\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}+2\zeta_{3}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{120}\gamma_{E}^{5}+\frac{1}{12}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{40}\bigg(9\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{5}\zeta_{5}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{240N}\bigg(20\gamma_{E}^{3}+5\gamma_{E}^{4}+30\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}+27\zeta_{2}^{2}+40\zeta 3+20\gamma_{E}\Big(3\zeta_{2}+2\zeta_{3}\Big)\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{6}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{720}\gamma_{E}^{6}+\frac{1}{48}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{9}{80}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{61}{560}\zeta_{2}^{3}+\frac{1}{18}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{18}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{E}\zeta_{5}-\frac{1}{240N}\bigg(5\gamma_{E}^{4}+\gamma_{E}^{5}+10\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}+27\zeta_{2}^{2}+20\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+10\gamma_{E}^{2}\Big(3\zeta_{2}+2\zeta_{3}\Big)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{E}\Big(27\zeta_{2}^{2}+40\zeta_{3}\Big)+24\zeta_{5}\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{7}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5040}\gamma_{E}^{7}+\frac{1}{240}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{5}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{3}{80}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{61}{560}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}^{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{72}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{12}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{3}{40}\bigg(\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{18}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{3}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{10}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{1}{10}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{1}{7}\zeta_{7}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{10080N}\bigg(42\gamma_{E}^{5}+7\gamma_{E}^{6}+105\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{2}+549\zeta_{2}^{3}+840\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+140\gamma_{E}^{3}\Big(3\zeta_{2}+2\zeta_{3}\Big)

\displaystyle+21\gamma_{E}^{2}\Big(27\zeta_{2}^{2}+40\zeta_{3}\Big)+56\Big(5\zeta_{3}^{2}+18\zeta_{5}\Big)+42\gamma_{E}\Big(27\zeta_{2}^{2}+20\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}+24\zeta_{5}\Big)\bigg)\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{8}^{A}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{40320}\gamma_{E}^{8}+\frac{1}{1440}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{6}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{360}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{5}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{3}{320}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\gamma_{E}^{3}\left(\frac{\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}}{36}+\frac{\zeta_{5}}{30}\right)+\gamma_{E}^{2}\left(\frac{61\zeta_{2}^{3}}{1120}+\frac{\zeta_{3}^{2}}{36}\right)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{E}\left(\frac{3}{40}\zeta_{3}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{\zeta_{5}\zeta_{2}}{10}+\frac{\zeta_{7}}{7}\right)+\frac{1261}{22400}\bigg(\zeta_{2}^{4}\bigg)+\frac{1}{36}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{15}\bigg(\zeta_{3}\zeta_{5}\bigg)-\frac{1}{10080N}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{7}+7\gamma_{E}^{6}

\displaystyle+21\gamma_{E}^{5}\zeta_{2}+35\gamma_{E}^{4}\Big(3\zeta_{2}+2\zeta_{3}\Big)+7\gamma_{E}^{3}\Big(27\zeta_{2}^{2}+40\zeta_{3}\Big)+21\gamma_{E}^{2}\Big(27\zeta_{2}^{2}+20\zeta_{3}\zeta_{2}+24\zeta_{5}\Big)

\displaystyle+\gamma_{E}\Big(549\zeta_{2}^{3}+840\zeta_{3}\zeta_{2}+280\zeta_{3}^{2}+1008\zeta_{5}\Big)+549\zeta_{2}^{3}+280\zeta_{3}^{2}+378\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}+504\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}+720\zeta_{7}\bigg)\,.

(105)

Similarly
\hat{\Gamma}_{B}(x)
is given by,

\displaystyle\hat{\Gamma}_{B}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{B}_{k}(x)^{k+1}\,,

(106)

where
\gamma_{k}^{B}
are given as

\displaystyle\gamma^{B}_{k}

\displaystyle=\frac{\Gamma^{k}(1)}{k!}(-1)^{k}\,.

(107)

Here,
\Gamma^{k}(1)

is the
k
th derivative of the Euler gamma function. We find explicitly

\displaystyle\gamma_{0}^{B}

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}^{B}

\displaystyle=\gamma_{E},

\displaystyle\gamma_{2}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}+\zeta_{2}\bigg),

\displaystyle\gamma_{3}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\gamma_{E}^{3}+\frac{1}{2}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{3}\zeta_{3},

\displaystyle\gamma_{4}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{24}\gamma_{E}^{4}+\frac{1}{4}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{9}{40}\zeta_{2}^{2}+\frac{1}{3}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{3}\bigg),

\displaystyle\gamma_{5}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{120}\gamma_{E}^{5}+\frac{1}{12}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{40}\bigg(9\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{5}\zeta_{5},

\displaystyle\gamma_{6}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{720}\gamma_{E}^{6}+\frac{1}{48}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{9}{80}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{61}{560}\zeta_{2}^{3}+\frac{1}{18}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{6}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{18}\zeta_{3}^{2}+\frac{1}{5}\gamma_{E}\zeta_{5},

\displaystyle\gamma_{7}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{5040}\gamma_{E}^{7}+\frac{1}{240}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{5}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{3}{80}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{61}{560}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}^{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{72}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{12}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{3}{40}\bigg(\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{18}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{3}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{10}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{1}{10}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{1}{7}\zeta_{7}\,,

\displaystyle\gamma_{8}^{B}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{40320}\gamma_{E}^{8}+\frac{1}{1440}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{6}\zeta_{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{360}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{5}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{3}{320}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{4}\zeta_{2}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{36}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{30}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{3}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{61}{1120}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{2}^{3}\bigg)

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{36}\bigg(\gamma_{E}^{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{3}{40}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}^{2}\zeta_{3}\bigg)+\frac{1}{10}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{2}\zeta_{5}\bigg)+\frac{1}{7}\bigg(\gamma_{E}\zeta_{7}\bigg)+\frac{1261}{22400}\zeta_{2}^{4}+\frac{1}{36}\bigg(\zeta_{2}\zeta_{3}^{2}\bigg)+\frac{1}{15}\bigg(\zeta_{3}\zeta_{5}\bigg)\,.

(108)

Appendix E
Resummed Coupling constant

For performing the integration over
\lambda^{2}
used in App.
C
, we replace
a_{s}(\lambda^{2})
by

\displaystyle a_{s}(\lambda^{2})=

\displaystyle\bigg({a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\over l}\bigg)\Bigg[1-{a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\over l}\Bigg\{\frac{\beta_{1}}{\beta_{0}}\log(l)\Bigg\}+\bigg({a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\over l}\bigg)^{2}\Bigg\{{\beta_{1}^{2}\over\beta_{0}^{2}}(\log^{2}(l)-\log(l)+l-1)-{\beta_{2}\over\beta_{0}}(l-1)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\bigg({a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\over l}\bigg)^{3}\Bigg\{{\beta_{1}^{3}\over\beta_{0}^{3}}\bigg(2(1-l)\log(l)+{5\over 2}\log^{2}(l)-\log^{3}(l)-{1\over 2}+l-{1\over 2}l^{2}\bigg)+{\beta_{3}\over 2\beta_{0}}(1-l^{2})

\displaystyle+{\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\over\beta_{0}^{2}}\Big(2l\log(l)-3\log(l)-l(1-l)\Big)\Bigg\}

\displaystyle+\left(\frac{a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})}{l}\right)^{4}\Bigg\{\frac{\beta_{1}^{4}}{\beta_{0}^{4}}\bigg(\frac{7}{6}-2l+\frac{l^{2}}{2}+\frac{l^{3}}{3}+4\log(l)-5l\log(l)+l^{2}\log(l)-\frac{3}{2}\log(l)^{2}+3l\log(l)^{2}

\displaystyle-\frac{13}{3}\log(l)^{3}+\log(l)^{4}\bigg)+\frac{\beta_{4}}{3\beta_{0}}(1-l^{3})+\bigg(\frac{\beta_{2}}{\beta_{0}}\bigg)^{2}\Big(\frac{5}{3}-3l+l^{2}+\frac{l^{3}}{3}\Big)

\displaystyle+\frac{\beta_{1}\beta_{3}}{\beta_{0}^{2}}\Big(-\frac{1}{6}-\frac{l^{2}}{2}+\frac{2}{3}l^{3}-2\log(l)+l^{2}\log(l)\Big)+\frac{\beta_{1}^{2}\beta_{2}}{\beta_{0}^{3}}\Big(-3+5l-l^{2}-l^{3}-3\log(l)+5l\log(l)

\displaystyle-2l^{2}\log(l)+6\log(l)^{2}-3l\log(l)^{2}\Big)\Bigg\}\Bigg]\,,

(109)

where
l=1+\beta_{0}a_{s}(\mu_{R}^{2})\ln(\lambda^{2}/\mu_{R}^{2})
and
\beta_{i}
are the coefficients of QCD
\beta
function

\beta(a_{s})=-\sum\limits_{i=0}^{\infty}a_{s}^{i+2}\beta_{i}
, known to five loops.

Appendix F
QCD
\beta
functions

\displaystyle\beta_{0}

\displaystyle={11\over 3}C_{A}-{2\over 3}n_{f}\,,

\displaystyle\beta_{1}

\displaystyle={34\over 3}C_{A}^{2}-2n_{f}C_{F}-{10\over 3}n_{f}C_{A}\,,

\displaystyle=(-1)^{l}\Bigg[C_{F}^{2}n_{f}\frac{1}{(1-\omega)^{3}}\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}\Bigg\{\frac{32}{27}\omega_{l}\Bigg\}-C_{F}^{2}C_{A}\frac{1}{(1-\omega)^{3}}\frac{1}{\beta_{0}}\Bigg\{\frac{176}{27}\omega_{l}\Bigg\}\Bigg]\,,

(122)

where
\gamma_{E}
is the Euler-Mascheroni constant,

\omega=a_{s}\beta_{0}\ln\big(N_{1}N_{2}\big)
,
\omega_{l}=a_{s}\beta_{0}\ln\big(N_{l}\big)
for

and

.
Here

and
\varphi^{(3)}_{q,z^{\prime},3}=-16/9~\beta_{0}C_{F}^{2}
, see the main text.

Appendix I
Convolutions

Relevant formulae for convolutions of distributions
{\delta_{\xi}}
=
\delta(1-\xi)
,
\mathcal{D}_{\xi,i}
=
\Big[\frac{\ln^{i}(1-\xi)}{1-\xi}\Big]_{+}
and logarithms
\mathcal{L}_{\xi,i}
=
\ln^{i}(1-\xi)
in (N)SV limits are listed below.

\displaystyle\bm{\delta_{\xi}\otimes\delta_{\xi}}

\displaystyle=\bm{1}\,,
